# Bug ledger

Every known defect, its evidence, its fix and the commit that closed it. The founder's rule
(2026-10-03): fix every known bug, then audit, fix what the audit finds, and repeat until an
audit round confirms nothing above LOW and every LOW is fixed or accepted here with a reason.

Status: **OPEN**, **FIXED** (commit), **ACCEPTED** (with the reason), **REFUTED**.

## Round 0: the bugs known on 2026-10-03

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | HIGH | The DA layer provides no data availability: it erasure-codes a ~200-byte JSON (a block hash and a tx count), not the block; every peer batch is refused (a separate random DA key is checked against the node address); an unsigned "legacy" batch is accepted; no committee check; the DA epoch counter restarts at 0 on every boot and overwrites earlier rows; peer batches and the node's own share keys (`da_root_{epoch}`); the broadcast dials `127.0.0.1` for unknown peers | `da/src/lib.rs:141-182`, `:261-445`, `:520-600` (before the fix); S6 cluster: 2,595 of 2,595 batches refused (G4 draft) | Bind a DA root of the block body into the block header; sample against the QC-certified header; delete the signed-batch layer | FIXED (a2ae5f1, review fixes 57df7c8 0b92c5e ba8faf0): `BlockHeader.da_root` over `Block::body_bytes()` (all body fields, `anchor_hash` included), header domain V2; 2x Reed-Solomon (k <= 128 data shards of ~512 B), RFC 6962 tree, root binds the length; one check `Block::check_commitments` on sync, QC import, QC recovery and state-sync anchor; RPC `aincore_getDaStatus`/`aincore_sampleDA` on real blocks, `getShardProof` removed; verifiers `da::verify_sample` and SDK `verifyDaSample` against shared vectors from an independent Python reference (`da/reference`); the old sequencer, DA key and `DA_COMMIT`/`DA_SHARD` messages deleted, their keys classified dead. Encode cost measured (one 768 KiB body: NAS 17 ms, Pi 55 ms; 6 MiB: 148 ms, 641 ms), column-parallel, roots memoised by body hash. Needs the fresh genesis (header format) |
| B2 | HIGH | Unknown peer addresses default to `127.0.0.1` | `storage.get_peer_ip` default; a restarted S6 validator dialled its peers on 127.0.0.1 | G4 S1: addresses come from the authenticated session or the bootnodes; never a guessed loopback | FIXED (1b3f40e): the V4 fan-out and the reconnect loop skip a peer with no recorded address (never a guessed 127.0.0.1); witness and killed mutant in `dag::peer_address_tests`. Addresses from authenticated sessions alone come with G4 S1 (B3) |
| B3 | HIGH | No per-peer budgets, queues, size caps before decode, or gossip scoring; the legacy TCP channel opens a connection per message | G4 contract, "What exists today" | G4 S1-S7 | IN PROGRESS: S1 (1318e45): libp2p identity = node key, consensus over member-only sessions with frames capped before read, sync over sessions with answers bounded in bytes. S2-S4 (5cda3f3): 50 non-member inbound connections, per-peer token budgets for members and sync sessions, QC votes capped at 8 KiB before parsing. Inbox (40b7968): the swarm never waits on the node (64 MiB byte-bounded inbox, busy answers past quota). S6a (cb78a03): snapshot restore over sessions, budgets keyed by the session key, RPC counts sessions. Next: S5 gossipsub scoring and S7 flood harness on the NAS and Pi |
| B4 | MEDIUM | Participation is measured by leader slots only: a member that authors only its leader-round vertices and signs nothing else is paid in full | A4 second review MEDIUM-5 | Blocks carry the authors of their committed vertices, bound in the header; rewards and BW-6 use them | FIXED (615c8c8): blocks carry the author of each committed vertex (`committed_authors`, aligned with the sequence, bound by the sequence root and the body); the executor credits each bootstrap operator in the committee with the rounds a block closes and the committed vertices it authored, and an epoch's participation is the lower of its leader share and its vertex share. Witness: an operator authoring only in the rounds it leads forfeits. Signing (attestation) is not measured: a certificate holds only its first quorum of signatures, so a slower honest member would score low; ACCEPTED as such, the vertex share already requires an operator to keep up with every round |
| B5 | MEDIUM | Anchor rounds that commit no block: 9.1 % on TESTNET-V4 (415 of 4,556 anchor rounds to height 4,141, 2026-10-03), spread over every scheduled leader (NAS r1/r2 10.6 %, Pi r3/r4 8.0 % and 7.5 %), in runs of up to 4; block intervals mostly 5-7 s with a tail to 60 s. S6 earlier: ~15 % | `scratchpad/b5_analyze.py` over all V4 headers (the schedule reproduces 4,141 of 4,141 proposers). The engine already waits for the previous anchor's leader (PR-3, `T_LEADER_TICKS = 2` ticks of 3 s, `v4.rs` `tick`), so the cause is not a missing wait | Timestamp, per node, when each round's quorum and the leader's certificate arrive, for missed anchors; then fix what the trace shows (candidates: tick granularity versus the wait window, an event-driven propose on the leader's certificate) | FIXED (c09623f): the trace showed every missed anchor was skipped by its own leader: the other three members certified the anchor round before the leader's (unaligned 3 s) tick, so its current round was already the next and it never proposed the anchor. The leader now proposes an anchor it owes first (`Engine::anchor_owed`), waits for its certificate from then; independent review found no CRITICAL/HIGH, its three LOWs fixed; four witnesses, mutants killed. Measured on the S7a rehearsal (4 validators NAS+Pi): before, 8 of 138 anchors missed (5.8 %; 6 of 129 with the B22 link missing); after, 0 of 149, block intervals 4-7 s (the tail to 60 s is gone) |
| B6 | LOW | Storage per block, with almost every block empty: 188 database writes a block and 17.0 KB of database a block on TESTNET-V4 (1,052,302 writes and 91 MiB by `du` for 5,597 blocks, node r1, 2026-10-03); S6 measured ~10 KB. A full node keeps 100,000 blocks, an archive node all of them (~79 GB a year at this rate) | `aincore_getStateClassStats` and `du` of `data-r1` on the NAS; S6 measurement | Break the writes down by key family (a read-only copy of the database), drop or compact what an empty block need not write, and fix the per-byte factor B14 needs | FIXED (615c8c8) for growth without bound: measured on a 4-node cluster past the GC horizon (GC_DEPTH + RETAIN_SLACK = 100 rounds), an empty block leaves 9.2 KB a node, of which the block (1.6 KB) and the state-tree versions (1.7 KB) were already pruned by the retention window and 5.8 KB stayed forever: signing guards 2.2 KB, the QC under two keys 2.1 KB, vote and aggregation rows 1.4 KB, the anchor decision 0.1 KB; receipts also stayed. Pruning a block now takes its receipts and all of those rows (pinned heights kept), so a full node holds a bounded window. The DAG rows (vertices, attestations, certificates, ~30 KB a block) are deleted by GC 100 rounds behind. Within the window the QC is stored twice and vote rows outlive their QC (~3.5 KB a block, ~350 MB at 100,000 blocks): ACCEPTED, bounded, and the QC code is consensus-critical |

| B8 | HIGH | Post-quantum signatures do not work and are pre-standard: the mempool admits a Dilithium5 transaction only against a `pqc_pubkey_{sender}` row that nothing can write, and the executor accepts only a 64-byte Ed25519 signature with a 32-byte key, so a PQC transaction is always rejected at execution; the VM's PQC branch is never called. The scheme is pre-standard Dilithium (pqcrypto-dilithium 0.5), not FIPS 204 ML-DSA | `common/crypto` PQC; mempool 9,254-hex path (`core/mempool/src/lib.rs:338-440`); executor Ed25519-only check (`core/executor/src/lib.rs`, "2. Verify Signature (Sender)"); `core/vm_move/src/lib.rs:343` unreferenced | ML-DSA-65 (FIPS 204) with the key carried in the transaction, one verifier for every path | FIXED (f0c2cdc): `crypto::verify_tx_signature` (Ed25519 strict, ML-DSA-65 via aws-lc-rs pinned =1.18.1, C-only on every CPU); the scheme follows from the key and signature sizes; keys and signatures cross-checked against RustCrypto `ml-dsa` (same seed, same key; each verifies the other) on the NAS; `executor::admission::check_stateless` is the one tx check for mempool, vertex ingress and executor; CLI `pqc-keygen` and `--pqc-seed`; pqcrypto-dilithium, the VM's dead signature path and `pqc_pubkey_` (now dead) removed. SDK signing with ML-DSA-65 is not in aincore-js yet (G6) |

| B12 | MEDIUM | Vertex payloads are not validated: a validator can fill each of its vertices with up to 768 KiB of arbitrary strings, and every committed payload item goes into the block body, stored by every node for good and carried by sync and DA. It costs the validator nothing (no fee is paid by an item that is not a valid transaction) | `consensus/consensus/src/dag.rs` block build collects every non-evidence payload item into `block_txs`; `ingress_v4.rs` checks the vertex size only. Found while measuring B1's encode cost | A deterministic, stateless admission check on payload items (decodes, chain id, signature, sender, size: the mempool's stateless half, as one function) at vertex ingress and at block build | FIXED (f0c2cdc): Layer S of vertex ingress (and the boot re-check) refuses a vertex whose payload item is not equivocation evidence or a transaction passing `check_stateless`; the work is spent after the author's signature checks. Residual: a member can still fill its vertices with valid signatures from unfunded keys, which the executor refuses at no fee; bounded by its share of vertices, as in Narwhal/Sui |
| B13 | MEDIUM | The paymaster path authenticated against the wrong account: `paymaster` was verified as a public key but used raw as the payer address (no account sits at a raw key), verification was non-strict (a small-order key signs for anyone), and the mempool skipped the balance gate for any tx naming a paymaster without checking its signature, so `"paymaster":"deadbeef"` bought free admission | `core/executor/src/lib.rs` paymaster block (before the fix); mempool test `admission_skips_check_for_paymaster_sponsored_tx` admitted "deadbeef". Found while routing B8 | The paymaster is a public key, the payer its derived address; its signature verified by the one verifier at admission and execution; the gate checks the payer's balance | FIXED (f0c2cdc): `admission::check_stateless` verifies it and returns the payer; conflict tokens use the derived address; witnesses `a_paymaster_pays_from_its_derived_address`, `admission_checks_the_paymasters_balance`, `a_paymaster_without_a_valid_signature_is_refused` |
| B14 | MEDIUM | Nothing ties block bytes to node storage or prices them. The body is every committed payload item: the block gas ceiling (200M) only decides which transactions EXECUTE, and the ones over it stay in the body, so a block's bytes are bounded only by 2n x 768 KiB per anchor (n validators). Gas is the declared `gas_limit` (charged in full), wallets declare 5,000-10,000 for a transfer, so even the ceiling admits 20-40k transfers (~9 MB) a block; a 100 KiB transaction pays what a 400-byte one does. The documented minimum validator disk is 100 GB and a full node keeps 100,000 blocks (`AINCORE_BLOCK_RETENTION`), so ~500 KB a block on average is what that disk can hold | `dag.rs` builds the body from all `block_txs`; `executor` block gas ceiling ("kept N tx, dropped M"); SDK `gasLimit = 10000`, CLI transfer `5000`; `docs/NODE_OPERATOR_GUIDE.md` (100 GB minimum); `block_pruning_policy_from_env` (100,000). Found while sizing B8 | Two resources, as production chains meter them: (1) a per-byte charge in the gas a transaction must declare, so fees and B15's base fee see bytes; (2) a payload byte budget per round, split across the committee's vertices and derived from retention x minimum disk, enforced at vertex ingress (Layer S) so the body cannot exceed it. The numbers are derived in a design note before code | FIXED (0dfa3a9): a transaction declares `gas_limit >= BYTE_GAS (400) x its raw bytes` (`admission::intrinsic_gas`); `MAX_GAS_LIMIT` caps the execution part only; the block ceiling counts whole limits, so a body holds at most 200M / 400 = 500 KB of transactions; the body is the transactions that executed and paid (`BlockExecutionSummary::body`), and sync refuses a block whose transactions differ from the body it re-executes; `aincore_estimateGas`, the CLI, bench-tps and aincore-js compute the limit with `gas_limit_covering`. BYTE_GAS is derived in `docs/research/fees_and_block_resources.md`. The per-round byte budget at ingress was not needed: the ceiling now bounds the body. Free reservation of the ceiling by transactions that cannot pay is B16 |
| B15 | HIGH | Fees do not resist spam: the minimum gas price is 1 quanta (10^-18 AIN) and nothing raises it when blocks fill, so a full 200M-gas block costs its sender 2x10^-10 AIN; one funded key can keep every block full and crowd out every user (a liveness attack) | `MIN_GAS_PRICE = 1` (`core/executor/src/admission.rs`, was `lib.rs` and `mempool`); no base fee anywhere; the G5 contract has no fee decision. Found while sizing B14 | An EIP-1559 base fee: burned or kept per the G5 fee rule, it moves by at most 1/8 per block toward half-full blocks, so sustained full blocks multiply the price by 1.125 per block; transactions must offer at least the base fee. B14's byte charge makes byte spam move it too | FIXED (0dfa3a9): `sys:base_fee` follows EIP-1559 (target 100M, denominator 8, written only when it moves) above the genesis floor `sys:config:min_base_fee`; a transaction must offer at least it (mempool and executor); `gas_limit x base fee` is burned, the tip follows the fee rule; the V5 floor is derived (a full block at the floor costs one block of emission: 2,709,779,308 quanta per gas, a genesis-tool test); `aincore_getGasPrice` returns it |
| B16 | HIGH | Block space can be reserved for free. The block gas ceiling is spent on every transaction's DECLARED limit before execution, so a transaction that then fails its nonce, balance or base-fee check pays nothing yet still uses the ceiling. The mempool lets such transactions in: it checks each one's gas cost against the payer's balance alone (not the sum of the payer's pending ones), and it checks neither a stale nor a far-future sequence number. So a key holding one transaction's gas (10M gas at the B15 floor: ~0.027 AIN) can send twenty stale-nonce transactions each declaring ~10M gas; honest validators include them, they fill the 200M ceiling, fail, and push every paying transaction out of the block, at no cost. A validator can do the same with its own vertices | `core/executor/src/lib.rs` block ceiling pre-filter (`budget.saturating_add(cost) > MAX_BLOCK_GAS_LIMIT`, before `schedule_batches`); `core/mempool/src/lib.rs` `add_transaction` (balance gate per transaction, nonce dedup by `sender:seq` only). Found while checking B14's byte bound | Count only what executes against the ceiling, as Aptos's block gas limit does: reserve a batch's declared gas before it runs, keep what executes, release what does not. The mempool refuses a nonce below the committed one, bounds the gap above it, and gates the payer's pending total | FIXED (c89b57d): the whole class, both block resources. The ceiling and the object limit (N-2, which was spent while parsing, `break` and all) are spent per parallel batch by transactions that can pay: `can_pay` drops what `execute_transaction` refuses before the VM (price below the base fee, a sequence number not the sender's next, a payer short of `gas_limit x price`), the rest reserve their declared gas and objects (bounding the VM work by the ceiling), and a rare refusal after the check gives its share back. The mempool refuses a used sequence number and one `MAX_NONCE_AHEAD` (100, Aptos's `capacity_per_user`) or more above, and gates the payer on all its waiting transactions. Witnesses: twenty independent stale or unfunded 10M-gas transactions, eighty with 10,240 objects, a 200M full block executing exactly twenty, three mempool refusals; five mutants (no `can_pay`, no reservation, no stale check, no window, no waiting total) each turn a witness red |
| B17 | LOW | A state-sync test is timed by the wall clock: `value_parts_wait_out_busy_answers_without_starting_over` serves 1 MiB parts under a 1 s request timeout; on a loaded Pi a part outlived the timeout and the restore restarted, failing "each part once, in order" | Failed once on the Pi (round g4c, 2026-10-03), passed 5 of 5 on the NAS and in every earlier Pi round | Time the test by an injected clock, or size its timeout to the slowest host | FIXED (3a5fab2): each part has 3 s and waits 600 ms, so the whole restore (4.8 s) still outlives one timeout; 3 of 3 on the Pi |
| B18 | LOW | The SDK's `getNodeStatus` calls `aincore_nodeStatus`, which only the unused library API (`core/node/src/api.rs`) answers; the node binary serves `aincore_getStatus`, so the call fails against every real node, and `getNetworkInfo`'s fallback counted validators as `peers_count + 1` | `aincore-js/src/connection.ts:361`, `:610`; `core/node/src/api_local.rs` (no `aincore_nodeStatus`). Found while moving RPC peer counts to sessions (G4 S6) | Call `aincore_getStatus`; count validators from `committee_sessions` | FIXED (cb78a03) |
| B19 | MEDIUM | `VERTEX_REQ` is served but no node sends it: the V3 vertex fetch has no client since V4 pulls over its own signed requests (`v4/pull.rs`, RE-3). The server is an open sync-protocol surface that spends blocking RocksDB point lookups for anyone, up to 512 a second node-wide (burst 1,024), on the NAS's spinning disk, which is already the consensus bottleneck | `rg 'VERTEX_REQ:'` finds only the server and its tests; `consensus/consensus/src/v4/pull.rs` is the V4 fetch | Delete the server, its budget and its message types (a surface with no honest use is only attack surface) | FIXED (153a521): server, H8 budget and message types deleted; `ChainSync::serves` refuses `VERTEX_REQ:` (witness in `the_node_routes_the_restore_requests_to_chain_sync`); `SyncRequest` also lost its unread, self-declared `sender_id`/`sender_port` |
| B20 | LOW | Test databases are never deleted: the state-sync and other suites leave every temp database behind (1,253 directories, 1.7 GB in the NAS's 1.9 GB `/dev/shm` after four runs), so a later run can fail on a full disk for no code reason | NAS `/dev/shm`, 2026-10-03 | Remove a test's database when the test ends, in one shared helper the suites use | FIXED (7ba4c26): `storage::test_dir::process_dir()` (<temp>/aincore-tests/<pid>) reaps the directories of exited test processes on first use; all 56 test paths go through it; witness `no_test_path_bypasses_the_reaped_directory` scans every crate. Workspace run on the NAS left 3.9 MB in `/dev/shm` |
| B21 | MEDIUM | A transaction sent to a node outside the committee is never propagated: `aincore_sendTransaction` only adds it to the local mempool, only members build vertices, and no node publishes transactions (the gossip `TX:` path had a receiver and no sender). Today it works only because the public RPC is a socket proxy to validator r1, which puts a validator's RPC on the internet; an RPC tier of observers (Aptos public fullnodes, Sui fullnodes) cannot accept transactions | `core/node/src/api_local.rs` `aincore_sendTransaction`; `rg '"TX:'` finds only the receiver; NAS `aincore-rpc-public.service` = `systemd-socket-proxyd 127.0.0.1:8201` | A forwarding protocol for observers: batches of transactions to one member chosen by the sender's hash (the next if it is not connected), each answered with the member's mempool verdict, under a per-session budget; gossip `TX:` refused (G4 S5) | FIXED (a17e4ec; gossip `TX:` refused in 3c76186): `node::forward` loans an outsider's mempool and sends `TX_SUBMIT:` batches to the member the sender's hash picks, failing over in order; per-transaction verdicts; mutants killed. Live on the S7a rehearsal (4 validators NAS+Pi, observer d5): a transfer sent to the observer's RPC was included at height 6 on validator d1, status success, recipient balance 5 |
| B22 | HIGH | A restarted node may never reach some committee members: (1) the libp2p addresses it saved were reloaded as bootnodes and mapped to +100 again (d1 dialled d4 at 9614, not 9514); (2) a peer's loopback address from identify was saved (d4 dialled d1 at 127.0.0.1); (3) a bootnode without a PeerId was dialled once at boot, so a member that booted later was never reached (the legacy reconnect loop removed in S6b used to retry); (4) Kademlia reported the wrong addresses back and they were saved again | S7a rehearsal 2026-10-03: after a restart d1 and d4 held two committee sessions instead of three; d1's log shows `Adding bootnode .../tcp/9614/p2p/<d4>` and dial errors; their leader slots were the only missed anchors (d1 12.5 %, d4 6.1 %) | Map operator bootnodes once; dial saved addresses as they are; save only what a dial reached and another host can dial; redial bootnodes without a PeerId until reached | FIXED (dc4c84d, save sites in the follow-up): `boot_dial_list`, `routable_for_others`, `Unresolved`; witness mutant reproduces the 9614 and 127.0.0.1:9613 dials. Live: after deploying, every validator held 3 committee sessions; with d4 down, d1 restarted and reached d4 5 s after d4 came back |

## Round 1: the audit of 2026-10-03 (three independent read-only reviews)

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B23 | CRITICAL | A forged QC halts every validator for good: a QC failing verification puts its own `chain_id` into the error text (`QcError` Display is its Debug), and `record_decision_conflict` halts when that text contains `DECISION_CONFLICT`; `QC_CERT` gossip and sync accept QCs from anyone; the alarm survives restarts | `consensus/consensus/src/qc_producer.rs` 639-643, 685-694, 782; `qc.rs` 143-146, 358-362 | Typed import errors: halt only on a conflict found after the QC verified, never on matched text (also `dag.rs` 1234, 1890 and `COMMITTEE_MISMATCH` in sync) | FIXED (229cdff): `consensus::alarm` — the code that finds a conflict raises a typed alarm on its thread; import, local acceptance, adoption and sync's EP-4 check ask for it instead of matching text. Witness `v4_a_forged_qc_carrying_the_conflict_text_raises_no_alarm`; the old text match as a mutant writes the alarm |
| B24 | CRITICAL | Connections that never finish the handshake are unbounded and never time out: no transport timeout, every cap runs after Noise+yamux, no pending-inbound limit or IP admission rate (NI-2) | `core/node/src/p2p.rs` transport and `ConnectionEstablished` | Transport timeout; pending-inbound limits and a per-IP admission rate before the handshake | FIXED (3f0f7af): 10 s handshake timeout; `InboundGate` admits per IP before the handshake (4 unfinished, 2/s burst 16); `connection_limits` caps 128 unfinished in all. Live: of 10 silent connections from one IP, 4 held, all closed after 11.5 s |
| B25 | HIGH | gossipsub 0.47 queues without bound toward a peer that stops reading; non-members can join the mesh | libp2p-gossipsub 0.47.0 `handler.rs`; `sessions.rs` gossip config | Gossip between members only (non-members scored below the graylist); observers get blocks and QCs over sync | FIXED (d4e7c13): non-members scored −32,000 (2× Lighthouse's graylist): gossipsub never meshes with, sends to, or processes them; observers ask boundary QCs over sync. Witness `gossip_runs_between_members_only`; mutant (non-members scored 0) lets the stranger hear the member |
| B26 | HIGH | `QC_CERT`/`QC_WANT` gossip is accepted and relayed from anyone, capped at ~776 KiB though the consumer drops >64 KiB; it fills the inbox member pushes share, and a dropped push is still acknowledged | `sessions.rs` GOSSIP_CAPS, `dag.rs` 633, `p2p.rs` inbox | Members-only gossip (B25); QC_CERT cap 64 KiB; a push dropped by a full inbox is not acknowledged | FIXED (d4e7c13): members-only gossip; QC_CERT capped at 64 KiB (the consumer's bound); a push is acknowledged only once queued |
| B27 | HIGH | Free block space is still possible after B16: `can_pay` checks price, nonce and balance, but `execute_transaction` can refuse after the reservation without charging (object-load gas above execution gas; a paymaster with no account record; and others) | `core/executor/src/lib.rs` 3563-3576, 4809-4823, 4995, 5027 | One pre-charge predicate shared by mempool, `can_pay` and execution; stateless floors in `check_stateless`; refusals after reservation become charged aborts | FIXED (090f752): stateless floors (objects, publish) in check_stateless, so vertex ingress keeps such transactions out of blocks; implicit paymaster account; every other refusal after the reservation is a charged abort |
| B28 | HIGH | The paymaster's signature does not cover `gas_price` or `input_objects`: a sponsored sender sets the price up to the paymaster's balance (pre-existing) | `core/executor/src/admission.rs` 59-66 | Bind `gas_price` and `input_objects` in the paymaster message (fresh genesis) | FIXED (090f752): the paymaster signs SHA-256(PAYMASTER_AUTH_V2 + the sender's seven-field message); SDK updated; mutant (old message) accepts a raised price |
| B29 | MEDIUM | Replays of members' signed gossip after the 60 s duplicate cache exhaust their publisher budgets everywhere | `p2p.rs` publisher budget; `sessions.rs` duplicate_cache_time | Members-only gossip (B25) and a budget keyed on the relay | FIXED (d4e7c13): members-only gossip, and the publisher budget keyed by (relay, publisher) |
| B30 | MEDIUM | Memory is held per connection before membership is known: `read_frame` allocates the declared length at once; non-member consensus streams are read in full before the member check; sync responses held for non-reading requesters | `sessions.rs` read_frame; `p2p.rs` consensus inbound | Grow the buffer as bytes arrive; refuse non-member consensus streams before reading | FIXED (0854083): (1) `read_frame` grows its buffer as bytes arrive (64 KiB reserved first; a frame shorter than its prefix is refused); (2) `MembersOnly`: `/aincore/consensus/1` exists only on connections whose key names a member when they open, so anyone else's stream is refused at negotiation, unread; a connection opened under an older book is closed by the committee dial (every 5 s) and a new member is dialled again; requests queued for a peer that got a connection without the protocol fail at once; consensus streams per connection 32 -> 16; the admission behaviours run first in the swarm (a later denial left request-response state behind); (3) answers handed to non-members count against `OPEN_HELD_MAX_BYTES` = 64 MiB until written or failed; past it a snapshot part is told busy and anything else is refused. Witnesses in `core/node/tests/serving_bounds.rs` (one real node): a never-reading client's eight 9 MiB answers, 7 held and the 8th refused, all freed on close, and a reading client's freed while it stays connected; a non-member's 700 KiB consensus frame refused at negotiation (UnsupportedProtocols), and once the book names it the old connection closes and a new one is acknowledged. Mutants killed: no bound, no release on failure, no release when written, non-members not counted, consensus on every connection, `misfiled` naming nothing. Independent review: no panic path in `MembersOnly` against libp2p-swarm 0.45.1 / request-response 0.27.0 |

Witness `add_checked_does_not_verify_the_signature_again`; mutant (verify again) killed |

Witnesses `silent_sessions_cost_a_pass_one_short_timeout`, `sessions_that_never_serve_lose_their_slots`; mutants (60 s asks; no strikes) killed |

Witnesses `the_anchor_is_asked_for_until_a_pair_verifies` (a liar's QC with a bad signature; the honest peer answers without a pair three times), `anchor_rounds_without_a_pair_do_not_end_the_restore`; mutants (one retry; give up after one fetch) killed. ## Round 2: the audit of 2026-10-03 (four independent read-only reviews of d820278)

Network and RPC, consensus, execution and economics, sync and storage; each on a read-only snapshot. B50 was found by two reviewers independently. B64 is the rehearsal's regression from round 1.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B47 | HIGH | `aincore_verifyVDF` takes the client's iteration count unbounded: 2^64-1 makes the VDF reserve 128 GiB (the node aborts), 2^50 pins an RPC worker for good; a few calls freeze the RPC | `core/node/src/api_local.rs` verifyVDF; `common/crypto/src/vdf/mod.rs` 170, 185-188 | Bound the difficulty in the VDF itself; the RPC refuses anything above the chain's own | FIXED (5a514ba): `aincore_verifyVDF`, `aincore_verifyProof` (it answered "proof_accepted" for any bytes) and `aincore_aggregateBLS` (a placeholder) are removed (nothing on chain used them); `VDFEngine` refuses a difficulty above `MAX_DIFFICULTY` = 2^24. Witnesses `demo_crypto_methods_are_not_served`, `a_difficulty_above_the_bound_is_refused`; mutants (a demo method served again; the bound off) killed |
| B48 | HIGH | A Byzantine member that stops reading its gossip stream makes every honest node queue every message for it without bound (explicit peers always receive publishes; gossipsub 0.47's per-peer send queue is unbounded): memory grows until the honest nodes fail together | `sessions.rs` score_peer; `p2p.rs` explicit peers; gossipsub 0.47 `behaviour.rs` 626-630, `handler.rs` 98, 412 | Bound what is queued per peer and drop the peer that does not drain it | FIXED (5a514ba): gossip is removed from the node (Aptos, Sui and Narwhal run validators on direct sessions): a consensus broadcast is pushed on every connected member session, an addressed message on its member's, and what a member misses comes back by V4 pull and rebroadcast. No queue grows for a member that stops reading (request-response streams time out), and gossip's own surface goes with it (B25, B26, B29, B32, B38). Witness `the_node_offers_no_gossip_protocol` (a gossipsub stream is refused at negotiation) |
| B49 | HIGH | The interpreter's size-dependent opcodes are flat-priced (`CopyLoc`, `ReadRef` 1 gas; `Eq`/`Neq` 1; `Pack`/`Unpack` 5; `MoveTo` 500) and there is no memory quota: a ~0.03 AIN transaction deep-copies a 1 MB vector millions of times, the same minutes of CPU on every node, every block | `core/vm_move/src/gas.rs` 129-272 | Charge by abstract value size (Aptos/Sui schedules) and bound memory per transaction | FIXED (5a514ba): copies, reads, compares, resource reads and writes cost by abstract value size, one gas per 42 units (Aptos: 140 internal units per abstract unit against 5,880 for an `add`), packs and unpacks by field count; copies held at once are bounded by `MEMORY_QUOTA` = 10,000,000 units (Aptos `memory_quota`), credited on pop, compare, write and frame drop (a local overwritten is not credited, stricter than Aptos). Witnesses `copies_and_comparisons_cost_by_size`, `held_copies_are_bounded_and_popped_ones_credited`; mutants (flat copies; no quota) killed. Gas schedule change: fresh genesis |
| B50 | HIGH | A V4 snapshot restore cannot work past epoch 0: the epoch record (`consensus:epoch_start`, `epoch_active`, `gc_floor`) is Local, so neither chunks nor the bootstrap carry it; the restore fails at every pinned checkpoint of epoch >= 1, and one restored at the first boundary refuses every next block | `sync/src/state_sync.rs` 883-887, 967-1019; `common/storage/src/class.rs` 346; `qc_producer.rs` 104-109; the state-sync tests build V3 chains | Rebuild the epoch record from restored state (`sys:validator_set:epoch:{E}`, already cross-checked) and write it in the bootstrap; a V4 restore test | FIXED (5a514ba): on V4, the restore takes C_E from the restored state's `sys:validator_set:epoch:{E}` (the executor's record, which `stage_boundary` requires to equal every node's), refuses a checkpoint past epoch 0 that is not an epoch boundary, rebuilds E+1's record from the boundary block by `next_start` (the executor's record must agree), checks that QC(H_E) binds it (the EP-4 check), and writes it with `epoch_active` in the bootstrap. Witness `a_v4_restore_at_a_boundary_holds_the_next_epochs_record` (the state-sync suite gained V4 chains); mutants (no record written; committee read from the Local record) killed |
| B51 | HIGH | Pull answers are not tied to a request: a member's unsolicited `Msg::Resp` carries 64 junk certificates for wanted slots, each a full BLS aggregate verification under the consensus write lock (~25k/s at its budget); honest nodes stop ticking and the chain halts at n=4 | `consensus/consensus/src/v4.rs` 611-615, 863-874; `v4/pull.rs` 541-551; `ingress_v4.rs` 325-360 | Answers only from the peer asked, for the slots asked; one certificate per slot; strike on the first failure; check "held" before verifying | FIXED (5a514ba): (1) costly checks that fail (BLS aggregate and vote verifications) are counted per thread (`consensus::work`), and a source (the member its session names) whose messages fail `MAX_FAILED_CHECKS` = 8 in 10 s is not heard for 60 s; (2) a pull answer brings at most one certificate per request this node sent for the slot (`MAX_ANSWERS_PER_SLOT` = 4 owed); (3) a pushed copy of a certificate already held is not verified again. Witnesses `a_source_that_fails_costly_checks_is_muted`, `junk_answers_cost_one_check_per_request_sent`; mutants (no mute; answers unbound) killed |
| B52 | MEDIUM | A member's QC votes for heights the node does not hold are each written in a synced transaction and never pruned (any anchor round): ~400 fsyncs/s and ~8.6 GB/day from one member | `qc_producer.rs` 852-873, 926-1045, 530 | A window above the tip and a round bound; votes for unknown blocks in a bounded memory map; prune by round floor | FIXED (5a514ba): a finality vote counts only for a block this node holds, at that block's round, and only the header is parsed; a voter re-sends its vote every tick until the QC forms (`retry_qc_work`), so one that arrives early is counted later. Witness `a_vote_counts_only_for_a_held_block_at_its_round`; mutant (the round check off) killed |
| B53 | MEDIUM | A member that answers Accepted and drops the transaction censors every sender mapped to it first: each re-forward goes to the same member, and the requeue cap then drops the transaction for good | `core/node/src/forward.rs` 88-104, 194-197; mempool requeue | A re-forward goes to the next member | FIXED (5a514ba): a transaction loaned again (no block settled it within 30 s) is forwarded starting at the next member in its sender's order (`loan_attempts`). Witness `an_accept_and_drop_member_cannot_censor`; mutant (no rotation) killed |
| B54 | MEDIUM | RPC handlers do unbounded work under shared locks: `sendTransaction` verifies under the mempool lock (B31 split only TX_SUBMIT); unknown-hash lookups hash every pending transaction under it; `getDag` clones the DAG under the consensus lock; `getBlocks` returns 1,000 full blocks; hash lookups scan 500-1,000 blocks; `sampleDA` re-encodes | `core/node/src/api_local.rs` 612-643, 774-783, 1050-1055, 1088-1094, 1110-1188, 1686-1690, 1742, 1869, 2366 | Verify outside the lock; bound responses by bytes; indexed lookups | FIXED (5a514ba): `sendTransaction` verifies before the mempool lock (`check_admissible`, `add_checked`); pending lookups compare the hash each transaction got at admission (`pending_with_raw_hash`), nothing hashed under the lock; `getDag` returns the newest 256 vertices; `getBlocks` stops at 8 MiB. | B55 | MEDIUM | One shared inbox: a member's junk gossip under the size cap is accepted and relayed, fills the 64 MiB FIFO every member shares, and each item is parsed under the consensus lock (NI-3 asks for a queue per peer and type) | `sessions.rs` judge_gossip, Inbox; `p2p.rs` gossip arm | A fair queue per publisher in the inbox | FIXED (5a514ba): the inbox keeps a queue per source (a member's address, or the peer a boundary QC was asked of), hands them over in turn, caps a source at a quarter (16 MiB), and when full makes the longest queue give way. | B64 | MEDIUM | Round 1 regression found on the rehearsal: a dial by PeerId opens several connections (one per address) and the dialer keeps one, but the listener closed every connection above 2 the moment it opened, sometimes the one kept; the dialer redialled in a loop (~150 closes a node in five minutes, consensus pushes lost in flight). Round 1's reconnect of misfiled sessions made it frequent | Rehearsal 2026-10-03 logs (d2: 316 opens from d5, d5: 164); `p2p.rs` duplicate rule | Close extras only once they outlive a grace; no extra dial on reconnect | FIXED (5a514ba): connections to one peer above 2 are closed only once they outlive a 5 s grace, the newest first (at once only past 16); a misfiled session is closed and the committee dial reconnects it (no extra dial beside it). Witnesses `a_dialers_kept_connection_survives_its_dropped_siblings`, `connections_past_the_grace_are_trimmed_to_two` (a real node); mutants (immediate close; no enforcement) killed |
| B65 | HIGH | State writes are not priced by size: a resource write is a flat 500 gas (`MoveTo`) and nothing charges a transaction for the bytes its write set adds. A block of 200 M gas can add ~200 MB of state (10 MB per transaction), ~2.6 TB a day at 6.65 s blocks, against 15 GB free on the Pi | `core/vm_move/src/gas.rs` charge_move_to; `core/executor` (no write-set charge); `MAX_BLOCK_GAS_LIMIT` 200 M | Charge each transaction's write set: IO per write (Aptos `storage_io_per_state_slot_write` 895,680 and `per_state_byte_write` 890 internal units against 5,880 for an `add`: ~152 gas a write, 1 gas per ~6.6 bytes) and state growth per new byte (Ethereum prices new state at 200-625 gas a byte against 3 for an `ADD`, Aptos at ~680 `add`s at its minimum price); at most 1 MB a write and 10 MB a transaction (Aptos limits) | FIXED (757dab1): every write to a `State` key pays I/O (152 gas a write and 1 per 6 bytes: Aptos's storage I/O over its `add`), and every state byte it adds pays the state byte gas: a new key its key, its value and 492 bytes of tree rows (measured by `measure_tree_bytes_per_key`), an existing key its growth; deletes and shrinks refund nothing (EIP-3529). The byte gas follows demand as the base fee does: target 716 charged bytes a block (10 GB of disk a year), at most +1/8 a block, floor 510 (EIP-8037's 1,530 gas a byte at 3 gas an `ADD`). Move execution stays capped at MAX_GAS_LIMIT and the writes are paid from the rest of the limit, which the block ceiling caps (EIP-8037's split). The charge's own writes are priced before the VM runs, in `can_pay` too, so every transaction the VM runs pays (an independent review found that the first version refused after the VM: free execution). One block adds at most 392 KB (it was ~200 MB). `aincore_estimateGas` dry-runs a whole transaction, two at most at once, on the blocking pool, with new bytes priced two blocks ahead; the CLI (`--execution-gas` overrides it) and the SDK use it. Witnesses `a_transaction_pays_for_the_state_bytes_it_adds`, `only_added_state_bytes_are_charged_and_nothing_is_refunded`, `the_state_byte_gas_follows_demand`, `a_block_over_the_state_target_raises_the_byte_gas`, `move_execution_is_capped_apart_from_the_writes`, `the_charges_own_writes_are_priced_before_anything_runs`, `the_gas_estimate_covers_the_writes_a_transaction_makes`, `dry_runs_are_bounded`; mutants (growth uncharged, no tree rows, whole value charged, every write counted, history charged, no I/O, rise uncapped, byte gas never written, block growth uncounted, VM uncapped, old admission cap, charge writes unchecked, can_pay ignores the charge, final check off, base fee required of estimates, estimate omits writes, one-block margin, dry runs unbounded) killed. Research note section 4. A gas schedule and admission change: fresh genesis |
| B66 | MEDIUM | Objects (`obj:` keys, accounts among them) store `data: Vec<u8>` as a JSON array of numbers: about 3.5 bytes of state per byte. An Ed25519 account is ~1,213 bytes, an ML-DSA-65 one ~14 KB, so under B65's state gas a first transaction costs ~3.5x what its content needs, and a new user's onboarding is ~2.5 state targets (independent review of B65, 2026-10-04) | `common/storage/src/object.rs` `Object::data` | Store `data` compactly (its UTF-8 text as a JSON string when it is text), at the fresh genesis | FIXED (04b10cb): `Object::data` is stored as its UTF-8 text when it is text (an account record is JSON), else as its byte array: one encoding a value, both forms read (`compact_bytes`). The CLI reads nonces from `aincore_getAccountNonce` and `data` in either form; the SDK already did. Witness `object_data_is_stored_compactly_with_one_encoding`; mutant (array always) killed, and under it the block-crash fixture reproduces the old root ba90d7a8... exactly, so the new pin 9e6887ef... moved through the encoding alone. Clients that read `data` as an array must accept text. Fresh genesis |

## Round 3: the audit of 2026-10-04 (four independent read-only reviews of 5fc1b9e) and the G5 rehearsal

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, storage, crypto and genesis; each on a read-only snapshot. B67 is the rehearsal's (all five nodes on the NAS). The consensus reviewer found the B51 mute not weaponizable; the network reviewer found the path through buffered vertices (B68).

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B67 | MEDIUM | The per-host cap on non-members counted connections: an observer's dial by PeerId opens one per address and keeps one, and its third connection, often the kept one, was closed as it opened; it redialled ~70 times a minute a validator (G5 rehearsal, 2026-10-04) | `p2p.rs` inbound per-host rule | Count identities per host; leave a peer's own extras to the per-peer grace | FIXED (949b358): the cap counts the non-member identities a host holds (`MAX_INBOUND_PEERS_PER_HOST` = 2); an identity's own extras fall under the per-peer duplicate rule (5 s grace). Witness `a_strangers_own_connections_are_not_counted_as_new_identities`; mutants (connections counted; no identity cap) killed. Rehearsal: the observer's connections stay flat (was +72 a minute) |
| B68 | HIGH | A Byzantine member can get honest members muted by B51: it buffers a vertex whose refs carry junk embedded certificates; when an honest member's certificate wakes it, the re-evaluation's failed BLS checks are charged to that member's message. Framing a different member on each node stalls a 4-member chain ~60 s in every ~70 s; a PendingEpoch vertex is re-evaluated at activation the same way | `dag.rs` 1775-1808; `v4.rs` 667-680, 990-1007; `ingress_v4.rs` 325-361 | Charge re-evaluation failures to the vertex's own deliverer (or nobody); drop failed embedded certificates before buffering; witness through the wake path | FIXED (11092e8): `work::uncounted` runs what a message wakes (a buffered vertex re-evaluated when a certificate arrives, E+1's buffered messages at activation) without charging its failures to that message; an embedded certificate that failed is stripped before the vertex is buffered, so a later wake does not pay for it again. Witnesses `a_waking_certificate_is_not_charged_for_the_vertex_it_wakes`, `junk_buffered_for_the_next_epoch_is_not_charged_at_activation`; the strip and activation mutants killed. The wake's `uncounted` is defence in depth: with the strip, no check left on a woken vertex can fail, so its mutant is equivalent (it survives) and the strip is what the wake witness kills |
| B69 | HIGH | Loading and verifying Move modules costs no gas and dependency depth is unbounded (`MoveVM::new`, default `VMConfig`): a published closure of modules is re-verified, unmetered, on every block (a fresh VM per block) by one cheap call; deep chains risk the stack | `vm_move/src/lib.rs` 214; `executor/src/lib.rs` 1726, 3379 | A production verifier config (Aptos: depth 256), caps on dependencies per transaction (Aptos 768 modules, 1.8 MB) and gas per dependency byte over the static closure (deterministic, not cache hits) | FIXED (11092e8): the VM runs with Aptos's production verifier limits (loop depth 5, generics 32, parameters 128, basic blocks 1,024, type nodes 256, stack pushes 10,000, structs 200, fields 64, functions 1,000; dependency depth 100), and a transaction pays for the modules it loads before the VM runs: 127 gas a module and 1 per 14 bytes over its closure (dependencies and friends, transitively; Aptos's `dependency_per_module` 744,600 and `dependency_per_byte` 420 internal units over 5,880 an add), refused past 768 modules or 1.8 MB. Witnesses `a_call_pays_for_the_modules_it_loads` (closure measured: blob 389 + signer 130 bytes), `a_closure_past_the_limits_is_refused_before_the_vm`, `the_vm_runs_with_the_production_verifier_limits`; three mutants killed |
| B70 | HIGH | Transactions that can never execute fill the mempool for free: future nonces (next+1..next+99), over 128 input objects or a price under the base fee are admitted, kept to `MAX_PENDING_TXS`, pulled into vertices by `gather_payload` without a re-check and skipped by `can_pay` (nothing charged); a full mempool refuses everything new. One payer censors all new transactions | `mempool/src/lib.rs` 285-338; `dag.rs` 1376-1386; `admission.rs` | Offer consensus only ready transactions (contiguous nonces); park the rest with their own bound; evict by price when full; the object cap in `check_stateless`; re-check price and nonce at gathering | FIXED (11092e8): a block is offered only READY transactions (contiguous from the committed sequence number over waiting and loaned, at least the base fee); a full pool evicts a parked one for a ready one and refuses a parked newcomer; a used sequence number leaves the pool; more than 128 input objects is refused at admission. Witnesses `only_ready_transactions_are_offered_to_a_block`, `a_full_pool_evicts_a_parked_transaction_for_a_ready_one`, `a_used_sequence_number_leaves_the_pool`; four mutants killed |
| B71 | HIGH | Governance escrows a voter's whole balance less 1 AIN and releases it only when the proposal executed: a failed or quorum-less proposal locks every voter's coins forever (the winning "no" side too); one escrow is shared across proposals; `GovernanceState` grows without bound and is read in full by every call. Confirmed by the 2026-08-12 audit (`governance-escrow-sink`) but never in this ledger | `governance.move` 86-88, 111, 140-189 | The voter chooses the weight; escrow released at resolution or deadline whatever the outcome; per-proposal escrow; bound and prune proposals and voters | FIXED (11092e8): a vote locks the amount the voter chooses, per proposal (`Lock`), at most 16 open; voting lasts 7 days of consensus time (Aptos mainnet `voting_duration_secs`); `claim_vote_tokens` returns every lock whose voting ended or was resolved, whatever the outcome; `execute_proposal` only after voting; descriptions at most 1 KiB; the global voters list is gone. SDK `vote` takes the amount, `claimVoteTokens` added. Witness `test_governance_vote_escrow_locks_real_coin_without_supply_drift`; the old governance.mv turns it red. Needs the fresh genesis |
| B72 | MEDIUM | B57 lets an unverified QC defeat the tip-agreement gate: a QC naming an epoch the node holds no committee for is counted "ahead" without a signature check, and one such seed lets the pass go on with fewer than `tip_agreement_n` verified tips | `sync/src/lib.rs` 636-648, 832-857 | Go on only when verified tips plus ahead claims reach `tip_agreement_n` | FIXED (11092e8): the pass goes on past an unknown epoch only if the verified tips and the ahead claims together reach N, and the verified tips agree. Witness `one_seed_past_an_unknown_epoch_does_not_waive_the_agreement`; mutant killed |
| B73 | MEDIUM | The sender's signature does not cover the paymaster: a relay can strip `paymaster` and its signature and the sender pays in full. The transaction id is SHA-256 of the raw JSON, so a relay can re-encode (key order, whitespace, unsigned `args`) to change the id a wallet tracks | `admission.rs` signing_message; `executor/src/lib.rs` 1236 | Sign the payer (or "self"); id over the canonical signed fields | FIXED (11092e8): the class, every byte a relay could change: the sender signs an eighth field, the paymaster's key, when sponsored; `args` must be empty, a paymaster signature needs a paymaster, keys, signatures and object ids are lowercase hex; nodes keep, forward and order transactions only in `admission::canonical_json` (vertex ingress refuses any other encoding), so the id (the hash of those bytes) and the byte gas are the sender's. SDK signs the paymaster and already emits the canonical order. Witnesses `a_relay_cannot_change_what_a_transaction_is`, `the_pool_keeps_the_canonical_encoding`, `layer_s_refuses_what_every_node_refuses`; six mutants killed. Needs the fresh genesis |
| B74 | MEDIUM | The RPC rate limit is bypassable and its key map never shrinks: IPv6 clients rotate within a /64; the forwarded header's first element is client-set behind an appending proxy; requests through the socket proxy look like loopback and may set the header; actix-governor never prunes its map. The B54 scans accepted as "bounded by the rate limit" become unbounded | `api_local.rs` 2480-2547, 2577 | Key IPv6 by /64; trust the last hop the proxy appended; accept the header only from a configured proxy; prune or cap the map | FIXED (11092e8): the forwarded header is read only from the proxies the operator names (`AINCORE_RPC_TRUSTED_PROXIES`), its last hop taken; an IPv6 client counts by its /64; keys fall in 65,536 buckets by a per-process random hash, so the limiter's map is bounded. Witness `the_rate_limit_counts_each_client_behind_a_proxy`; three mutants killed. Operators behind a proxy must set both variables |
| B75 | MEDIUM | Non-member slots and held-answer bytes are first-come: 25 IPv4 hosts at 2 identities each take all 50 non-member inbound slots and keep them with a GET_HEIGHT every 15 s (no eviction, no allowlist), shutting observers and the public RPC tier out; two non-reading hosts fill the shared 64 MiB held-answer pool | `p2p.rs` 194, 405-411, 713-735; `sessions.rs` 564, 918 | Reserved peers (operator allowlist), eviction of idle non-members, a per-/24 limit, a per-host share of the held-answer pool | FIXED (11092e8): the 50 non-member slots evict as Bitcoin Core does (`SelectNodeToEvict`: from the network group, /16 IPv4 or /32 IPv6 as `NetGroupManager::GetGroup`, with the most connections, the youngest, when it holds two more than the newcomer's group); the operator's reserved peers (`AINCORE_RESERVED_PEERS`) are never counted; each group holds at most 16 MiB of the 64 MiB held-answer pool. Witnesses `a_full_table_makes_room_for_new_network_groups`, `one_group_cannot_hold_the_whole_pool`; two mutants killed |
| B76 | MEDIUM | `getDag` is bounded by count, not bytes (256 bodies of up to 768 KiB, ~192 MiB), and clones and serializes them while holding the consensus read guard and the DAG mutex, so consensus writers wait | `api_local.rs` 15, 1091-1104 | Cap in bytes as `getBlocks` does; copy under the lock, serialize after | FIXED (11092e8): `getDag` stops at 8 MiB of payload (one vertex at least), copied under the locks and serialized after them. Witness extends `dag_and_block_answers_are_bounded`; mutant killed |
| B77 | MEDIUM | Loops that take the consensus `std::sync::RwLock` run on tokio workers (ticker, inbox consumer, syncs): during a block they park most workers and can starve the swarm task. A panic under the lock poisons it, the loops skip with `if let Ok` forever and the process stays up (systemd never restarts it) | `main.rs` 332, 880, 932, 1011, 1067 | Run them on dedicated threads (or spawn_blocking); abort the process on panic | FIXED (11092e8): a panic anywhere aborts the process (systemd restarts it), so no loop ever skips a poisoned lock; the ticker, the inbox consumer and the post-sync reloads hold the consensus lock inside `block_in_place`, off the async workers. Witness `a_panic_on_any_thread_ends_the_process` (a child process, SIGABRT); mutant killed |
| B78 | MEDIUM | An unused Merkle accumulator appends every block hash and recomputes its root over all leaves (~2N hashes) under the consensus write lock; the root is never read and the leaves grow for the process's life (~0.4 s a block after 30 days on the Pi, by the reviewer's estimate) | `dag.rs` 111, 349, 1333-1336; `crypto/src/accumulator` | Delete it | FIXED (11092e8): the accumulator is deleted from the DAG (it was never read) |
| B79 | MEDIUM | Testnet scoring (which feeds the mainnet genesis) builds funding clusters from top-level `coin::transfer` calls only: paymaster-sponsored accounts, module-mediated `coin::withdraw`/`deposit` and zero-amount transfers make Sybils their own clusters and earn points, evading the 1,000 AIN per-cluster cap; the first transfer of any coin decides an account's funder | `genesis-tool/src/score.rs` 145-177, 378-414, 431-445 | Only AIN transfers above 0 count; the paymaster funds what it sponsors; edges from AIN balance changes in write sets, not call shapes | FIXED (11092e8): funding edges are AIN transfers of some amount and a paymaster's sponsorship; an account whose funding does not lead to the faucet is `unattributed`, one cluster capped once. Fail-closed without write sets: an honest account funded only through a module shares that cap. Witness `sybils_outside_the_funding_tree_share_one_cap`; three mutants killed |

Witness `a_server_without_a_blocks_qc_does_not_stall_the_pass` (a pass with one session per pass stalls); mutant killed |
| B81 | LOW | A member's `QC_WANT` is answered by broadcasting `QC_CERT` to every member, n sends where one is asked | `dag.rs` 623-662 | Answer the asker | FIXED (11092e8): a member's `QC_WANT` is answered on its own session (`Outbound::To`); an ask with no named source is answered to everyone. Witness `a_qc_want_is_answered_to_its_asker`; mutant killed |
| B82 | LOW | The restore's bootstrap record leaves `next_anchor_round`, `gc_floor` and the committed sequence to derivation (safe while a restored node only adopts) | `state_sync.rs` 995-1037 | Write them and assert the observer constraint | FIXED (11092e8): the restore writes the GC floor (the restored anchor round) and the next anchor round, and checks again that its key is no validator of the restored chain (SN-6). Witness: `assert_restored` checks both rows; mutant killed |
| B83 | LOW | `deposit_fee_reward` ignores the Move abort status: an abort returns Ok with no writes, so a recipient without a coin store loses its share silently and the supply trackers overstate (the sweep queue is unreachable) | `executor/src/lib.rs` 3133-3156 | Treat an aborted status as an error | FIXED (11092e8): an aborted `deposit_fee_reward` is an error. Witness `an_aborted_fee_deposit_is_an_error`; mutant killed |
| B84 | LOW | The estimate's executor (and VM) lives as long as the node: its module cache is never invalidated, so estimates run old code after an upgrade, and it grows with every module estimated | `main.rs` 724; `api_local.rs` 1785 | A fresh VM per estimate, or invalidate on publish | FIXED (11092e8): each estimate runs on a fresh executor and VM, as a block does. | B85 | LOW | The DEX pool registry is permissionless and one vector every swap scans: swap gas grows linearly with the pools anyone creates (state gas now prices their creation) | `dex.move` create_pool, assert_registered_pool | Key pools by pair (a table) or bound the registry | FIXED (11092e8): a pool's registration is its `LiquidityPool<X, Y>`'s existence (only `create_pool` makes one, and none is deactivated), checked in constant time; the registry holds at most 256 pools. | B86 | LOW | Consensus Ed25519 checks are non-strict and genesis accepts small-order keys: a genesis member with an identity-point key makes (R = identity, s = 0) verify for every message, so anyone can author its vertices and blocks or forge its equivocation | `blockchain/src/lib.rs` 198, 966; `crypto/src/lib.rs` 162; `executor/src/lib.rs` 4114; `multi_genesis.rs` 110; `genesis.rs` 122; `committee.rs` 69-75 | `verify_strict` everywhere; refuse weak keys at genesis and in committees | FIXED (11092e8): `crypto::verify_signature`, block and vertex signatures, AA and genesis entry signatures verify strictly; genesis and committee validation refuse small-order keys (`crypto::ed25519_key_is_usable`). Witnesses `a_small_order_key_signs_nothing`, `a_small_order_member_key_is_refused`, the block and vertex forgery in `proposer_signature_binds_block_to_proposer_key`, genesis in `a_bootstrap_genesis_weighs_s_min_and_mints_nothing`; four mutants killed |
| B87 | LOW | `node.key` is written with the process umask (0644) and narrowed to 0600 afterwards, the error ignored | `main.rs` 411-421 | Create 0600 exclusively, write, fsync, rename; fail if permissions cannot be set | FIXED (11092e8): node.key is written to a temporary file created 0600 and exclusively, synced, renamed, and the boot fails if it is not 0600. Witness `a_secret_file_is_created_owner_only`; mutant killed |
| B88 | LOW | A member can hold all 8 serving permits with 8 MiB sync answers (the byte charge and held-answer bound apply to non-members only), starving observers | `main.rs` 773-776; `p2p.rs` 415-421 | Members keep their own permits; charge members by bytes too | FIXED (11092e8): members keep to their own four serving slots, and their answers are charged by bytes as non-members' are |
| B89 | LOW | A broadcast clones the wire string for every member (up to ~776 KiB each, ~194 MiB at 256 members) and keeps the copies while slow members read | `p2p.rs` 330-337 | Shared bytes in the codec | FIXED (11092e8): the consensus codec carries `Arc<str>`, so a broadcast hands every member the same bytes |
| B90 | LOW | Kademlia runs though nothing uses it (stores anyone's records, bootstraps every 5 minutes dialling peer-supplied addresses), and `aincore_getPeers` gives any RPC client the saved validator addresses | `p2p.rs` 121-126; `api_local.rs` 1251 | Remove Kademlia (or client mode); keep validator addresses off the public RPC | FIXED (11092e8): Kademlia is replaced by `sessions::AddressBook` (bootnodes, dialled addresses and sessions' identify addresses, 8 a peer, 1,024 peers): no records, no queries answered, no bootstrap dials; `aincore_getPeers` names sessions (PeerId, member or not), never an address. Witnesses `the_address_book_is_bounded_and_dials_what_it_holds`, `get_peers_gives_no_addresses`; two mutants killed. A new member must name the committee in its bootnodes (G4: addresses come from sessions or the operator) |

## Round 4: the audit of 2026-10-06 (four independent read-only reviews of 82d39c7)

Consensus and sync, execution and economics, network and RPC, and an adversarial review of round 3's fixes. Three HIGHs are regressions of round-3 fixes (B92 of B77, B93 of B69, B96 of B81): the adversarial review is what found two of them.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B92 | HIGH | B77's abort-on-panic hook aborts before unwinding, so every `catch_unwind` placed on purpose stops working: the state tree's `no_panic` (jmt unwraps on a pruned sibling: a proof RPC at the floor racing a prune; snapshot serving; a hostile restore server) and the Move verifier and deserializer, which catch their own panics. A verifier panic on a published bundle would crash-loop every validator re-executing that block | `node/src/lib.rs` hook; `state_commit/src/lib.rs` `no_panic`; move `verifier.rs:89`, `deserializer.rs:47` | The hook leaves a panic inside `storage::panic_guard::caught` or in the VERIFIER/DESERIALIZER state to unwind (Aptos's crash handler spares the same states) | FIXED (9590d9d): the abort hook spares a panic caught on purpose: one inside `storage::panic_guard::caught` (the state tree's `no_panic` runs through it) or while the Move VM is in its VERIFIER or DESERIALIZER state (the two states Aptos's crash handler spares). Witnesses `a_panic_on_any_thread_ends_the_process` (the child process checks both catches stay caught and prints a marker the parent requires before the SIGABRT), `a_tree_panic_is_marked_as_caught`; three mutants killed |
| B93 | HIGH | B69 set the VM's `max_dependency_depth` (100), but the loader counts depth only through modules its block-wide cache does not hold yet: in a parallel batch (`coin::transfer<T>` with user types runs in parallel), whether a load passes depended on thread timing, so nodes could disagree on a block's writes (a fork) | move loader `load_and_verify_dependencies`; `vm_move/src/lib.rs` `vm_config`; `executor` parallel batches | No depth in the VM; the executor bounds the loader's recursion from storage (`deps::MAX_DEPENDENCY_DEPTH`, the longest path through the closure's components) before the VM runs | FIXED (9590d9d): the VM runs with no `max_dependency_depth`; the executor bounds the loader's depth from storage before the VM runs: the longest path through the closure's strongly connected components (a friend cycle counts as all its modules), at most 100 (move's suggested depth), the same on every node whatever its module cache holds. Witnesses `the_dependency_depth_is_bounded_from_storage` (100 loads, 101 refused, twice; the stdlib's friend cycles load), `a_cycle_counts_as_its_modules_and_the_deepest_branch_counts`, `the_vm_runs_with_the_production_verifier_limits`; four mutants killed |
| B94 | HIGH | The mempool admits transactions every block skips for free (B70 bypass): `gas_limit` equal to the intrinsic byte gas (execution 0) passes admission and the payer gate, is READY so never evicted, and `can_pay` skips it in every block without a charge (requeued three times, then resubmitted at price+1); value moved out of the payer has the same effect on its later transactions | `mempool` `add_checked`, selection, `is_ready`; `executor` `can_pay` | One payability predicate (the pre-charge fits the execution gas; the payer covers the run's cumulative cost) at admission and at selection; what fails at selection is dropped | FIXED (9590d9d): one payability predicate, `executor::precharge_gas` (object loads and account writes at the committed byte gas, priced as if the sequence number were the sender's next): the mempool admits only a transaction whose execution gas covers it, and selection drops one that no longer fits and stops a sender's run where its payer's committed balance does not cover it. Witness `what_a_block_would_skip_is_neither_admitted_nor_offered`; four mutants killed |
| B95 | HIGH | Layer S (every payload transaction's signature) runs on every vertex copy before the cheap Layer E filters and with no per-(author, round) dedup: a member sends 768 KiB of one signed transaction repeated, each message at a new round beyond the lead window (dropped after the cost) or citing fabricated parents (pending, never staged, so no twin evidence), costing ~50-200 ms of signature checks under the consensus write lock per message; B51 counts failures only | `ingress_v4.rs` 198, 302, 309-325; `v4.rs` 624-695 | Epoch, floor and lead-window checks before the payload; at most two digests per (epoch, round, author) take the payload check; duplicate payload items refused | FIXED (9590d9d): Layer S checks the hash, signature, round and refs (`layer_s_head`); the payload's checks run after Layer E's cheap filters, gated per (epoch, round, author): a body checked before is not checked again, and a third plain body of one slot is dropped unchecked (a body this node holds a certificate for is always checked: a certified twin evicts a plain one); a repeated item is refused before any signature. The first version dropped a certified third twin too; the workspace run caught it (`a_certified_third_twin_evicts_the_plain_one_on_a_node_that_attested_another`) before commit. Witnesses `the_payload_is_checked_after_the_cheap_filters` (a bad payload past the lead is dropped unchecked), `a_third_body_of_one_slot_is_not_checked`, `an_engine_checks_two_plain_twins_of_a_slot_and_not_a_third`; six mutants killed |
| B96 | HIGH | B81 regression: the QC answer throttle is per height and the per-tick budget shared, and since B81 the answer goes to the asker only, so one member asking every height every tick takes the only answer; a slow honest member then cannot get QC(H_E) and cannot activate the next epoch (at n=4, with one Byzantine member, the chain halts) | `dag.rs` 622-667 | Throttle per (asker, height); members may also fetch a QC by height over sync | FIXED (9590d9d): the QC_WANT throttle and the per-tick budget are per asker (askers are committee members: only members' pushes reach the handler), so a member asking every height cannot take a slow member's answer. Witness `a_qc_want_is_answered_to_its_asker` (a greedy member asking four heights three times and a slow one, in one tick); two mutants killed |
| B97 | HIGH | AutoNAT runs on every node though nothing uses it, and every identify push carrying a new observed address adds a never-pruned candidate: one stranger's connection pushing 4 KiB identifies grows memory ~1 MB/s, and each push logs ~4 KB of its text (no log rotation in the compose files). Its server also dials back attacker-chosen ports | `p2p.rs` 130, 162, 828-839; autonat 0.13 `v1/behaviour.rs` | Remove AutoNAT; take identify from members only and bound its log | FIXED (9590d9d): AutoNAT is removed (nothing read it); identify reports are kept and logged only from committee members, the log line bounded (agent 64 chars, an address count). Swarm wiring: no unit harness |
| B98 | MEDIUM | B70 leftovers: eviction runs before the duplicate-nonce check (a resubmitted same-nonce transaction evicts an honest parked one and is then refused, free); `evict_parked` and selection read each sender's record under the mempool and consensus locks; parked transactions never expire; an honest head transaction priced under a risen base fee blocks its account (no replacement) | `mempool/src/lib.rs` 339-369, 476-497, 727-792 | Duplicate checks first, a free nonce slot to count as ready; one committed-sequence read per sender per pass; parked expiry; same-nonce replacement at a higher price | FIXED (9590d9d): a same sender:sequence is refused before anything else unless it pays 10 % more (geth's txpool `PriceBump`) and the old one is queued, then replaces it without evicting; parked transactions leave after 600 s (Aptos's `system_transaction_timeout_secs`); committed sequence numbers are read once per committed height (a block commits its accounts and `latest_height` in one transaction). Witnesses `duplicates_replacements_and_parked_expiry`, `the_committed_sequence_is_read_again_each_height`; five mutants killed |
| B99 | MEDIUM | `token_factory` keeps every token in one global vector with unbounded name, symbol and URLs, loaded whole by create, mint, burn and disable: ~50 tokens with ~49 KB fields (~5,000 AIN burned) push it past what `MAX_GAS_LIMIT` can load, and those calls abort for everyone, for good | `token_factory.move` 64-127, 140, 223, 326 | Bound the field lengths and the token count | FIXED (9590d9d): `token_factory` bounds name (64 bytes), symbol (16), URLs (256) and the registry (512 tokens), checked before the fee. Witness `market_bounds_tests` (a u64/u8 pool aborts EINVALID_PAIR, the AIN/wBTC pool is created); the round-3 dex.mv and a source mutant turned it red. Needs the fresh genesis |
| B101 | MEDIUM | B51 counts failed checks only: a replayed valid `QC_VOTE` costs a BLS verification before the (round, signer) dedup, re-verification of every collected vote, and a synced commit even when nothing is written; a replayed valid `QC_CERT` costs a pairing and a synced transaction; attestations are verified before their duplicate check and a bad one is never counted; collecting n votes re-verifies O(n^2) | `qc_producer.rs` 984-1024; `dag.rs` 673-690; `vcert.rs` 451-466 | Dedup before verifying; verify each vote once; skip empty commits; count a bad attestation | FIXED (9590d9d): a QC vote from a signer already held, or for a round with a QC, is skipped before its BLS check; collected votes are stored as `digest:signature` and counted by digest, not verified again (records from before are verified as they were); the node's own vote completes a QC when it is the last; a QC already stored, or for a height not held, is skipped before its pairing; an exact replay of a verified attestation needs no pairing and a bad one is a failed check (B51); a storage transaction that wrote nothing commits nothing (no witness: nothing observable but the skipped sync). The first version read the node's own `digest:signature` record as a bare signature, so a vote retried after a restart failed; the workspace run caught it (six qc_producer persistence tests) before commit. Witnesses `held_votes_are_not_rechecked_and_the_last_vote_completes`, `a_replayed_or_unheld_qc_costs_no_pairing`, `a_replayed_attestation_is_not_verified_again_and_a_bad_one_is_counted`, the qc_producer persistence tests; eight mutants killed |
| B102 | MEDIUM | B90's address book evicts in insertion order and takes any stranger's identify addresses, so churn evicts members (added first, never refreshed) and a member's link drop becomes permanent; AutoNAT confirms server-reported addresses unchecked, so a member advertises junk that pushes its real address out of others' books | `sessions.rs` 951-981; `p2p.rs` 498-505, 827-838 | Pin bootnode and dialled addresses; identify addresses for committee members only; never evict a committee peer; least-recently-used | FIXED (9590d9d): the address book pins bootnode and dialled addresses (never evicted), learns addresses only from members' identify and mDNS, and evicts the least recently heard unpinned peer. Witness `the_address_book_is_bounded_and_dials_what_it_holds` (the pinned bootnode and member stay, a peer that keeps reporting stays, a silent stranger leaves); two mutants killed |
| B103 | MEDIUM | B75's held-answer pool is still filled by four hosts in four groups (20 MiB each, non-reading 800 KB sync answers held 60 s), and the operator's reserved peers and `TX_SUBMIT` answers share it, so RPC observers, restoring nodes and forwarded-transaction verdicts starve | `sessions.rs` 1164-1170; `p2p.rs` 429-438, 576-582 | Reserved peers outside the pool; a group's share shrinks with the number of groups holding answers; `TX_SUBMIT` verdicts outside it | FIXED (9590d9d): the operator's reserved peers and `TX_SUBMIT` verdicts are outside the non-members' held-answer pool (still charged by bytes). Residual, accepted: four /16 hosts can still fill that pool for the other public non-members (members, reserved peers and forwarded transactions are not in it). Swarm wiring: no unit harness |
| B104 | MEDIUM | B74 trusts a forwarded header by peer IP alone: if a loopback socket proxy is reachable without Cloudflare, a client sets the header per request and escapes the rate limit (conditional on deployment) | `api_local.rs` 2510-2518 | Take the header only on a listener only the proxy reaches (a separate port) | FIXED (9590d9d): with `AINCORE_RPC_PROXY_PORT` set, the server also listens on 127.0.0.1 at that port and takes the forwarded header only from requests that arrived there (the proxy's own listener). Witness `the_rate_limit_counts_each_client_behind_a_proxy`; mutant killed. Operators behind a proxy point it at that port |

| B106 | LOW | SDK: `signingMessage` adds the paymaster when it is not undefined, `toString` only when truthy (an empty string disagrees); the package's `main` points at `dist/`, which still signs the old message | `aincore-js/src/transaction.ts`; `package.json` | One condition for both; rebuild `dist/` before publishing (founder-side release step) | FIXED (9590d9d) in the SDK source: one condition (`if (this.paymaster)`) for the signing message and `toString`. Witnesses `failing_sweeps_back_off_and_do_not_block_the_queue`, `test_fee_sweep_queue_recovers_after_miner_registers_coinstore`, `a_queued_fee_is_keyed_by_the_executing_height`; two mutants killed |
| B109 | LOW | In a charged abort, the prologue's rewrite of the payer's CoinStore pays no I/O gas | `executor` 5262-5280 | Price it with the account writes | ACCEPTED (9590d9d): the prologue's CoinStore rewrite is a fixed-size fee deduction (~167 gas at today's byte gas), far below any transaction's intrinsic gas, and the same on every node |
