## Round 5: the audit of 2026-10-06 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-4 tree)

Consensus and sync, execution and economics, network and RPC, and an adversarial review of round 4's fixes. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state. Both HIGHs are gaps in earlier fixes (B117 of B95, B116 of B56/B111), and B121 is a regression of B108 that the test seeding guard hid.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B116 | HIGH | B56/B111 incomplete: the pending-handshake slots kept for members work only for IPs already in `member_ips`, which starts empty at every boot (in memory), is filled only from authenticated member sessions, holds up to 1,024 IPs and is never pruned; member IPs are exempt from the per-IP rate and the bound on other IPs. (a) Cold start: 28 IPv4 hosts x 4 silent TCP connections (0.4/s per IP, under the 2/s rate, re-opened at the 10 s timeout) hold all 112 slots for other IPs, so every member's first dial is refused and no member IP is ever learned: the chain cannot start. (b) One Byzantine member authenticates from 32 addresses (recorded for good), then holds 4 unfinished handshakes on each: all 128 pending slots; honest members that restart or reconnect are refused (n=4: a halt) | `sessions.rs` 814-816, 874-898; `p2p.rs` 171, 181-184, 700-710 (round-5 network review) | Seed the kept slots at boot from the bootnodes and saved member addresses; one current IP per member PeerId (replaced on reconnect, dropped when it leaves the committee) so a member holds at most its own kept slots; bound other IPs' pending handshakes per network group | FIXED (46594c5): the gate's kept IPs (`KeptIps`) hold the boot dial list's IP literals (bootnodes and saved members) from the start, and each committee member's current IP, one per member PeerId, replaced when it connects from another and dropped once it leaves the committee; outside them, one network group holds at most `PENDING_PER_GROUP` (8) unfinished handshakes. Witnesses `the_kept_slots_hold_from_boot_and_one_ip_per_member`, `slots_are_kept_for_members_ips`; three mutants killed |
| B117 | HIGH | B95 incomplete: a payload that FAILS its checks is checked again on every copy and nothing counts it (only Stage/PendingCert/PendingEpoch bodies are remembered; payload failures never `note_failed_check`). A member signs one vertex (invented parents, ~1,000 sponsored transfers, the last invalid) and resends it at its message budget: ~2,000 Ed25519 checks per copy, 50-200 ms each under the consensus write lock, unbounded. Remainders: E+1 pending vertices are payload-checked with no round limit; payload_slots is lost at restart (bounded re-checks) | `ingress_v4.rs` 326-330, 381-384; `v4.rs` 633-643, 712-718, 760 (round-5 consensus review) | Count a failed payload check (B51) and record the refused body in its slot, so a slot takes at most two payload checks whatever their outcome; check the payload only on the staging path (a waiting vertex is checked when it wakes); E+1 pending vertices only up to first_round(E+1)+LEAD | FIXED (46594c5): the payload is checked only for a vertex that stages (or is buffered for E+1), last; a vertex waiting on parent certificates is checked when it wakes, so one on invented parents never costs a check; each check's outcome is remembered in its slot, passed or refused (a refused body is refused again unchecked, and a slot takes two checks whatever their outcome), and a refusal is a failed costly check (B51: the payload checks are stateless, so no honest node stages or serves a refused body). Witness `a_refused_payload_is_checked_once_and_a_waiting_one_not_at_all`; three mutants killed |
| B118 | MEDIUM | B95's `payload_slots` is unbounded through the PendingEpoch path: an E+1 vertex returns PendingEpoch before the lead, floor and cursor checks, and `parent_refs_admissible_above` needs only that the declared parent authors hold a next-committee quorum, not that the parents exist. A Byzantine member of the next committee sends empty-payload E+1 vertices at arbitrary rounds (up to `ABSOLUTE_ROUND_CEILING`); each inserts a slot the GC floor never reaches; past 65,536 the prune is an O(n) scan under the write lock on every vertex, then OOM (and B92's abort) on every honest validator | `v4.rs` 172, 648-664; `ingress_v4.rs` 381-384; `qc.rs` 248 (round-5 adversarial review) | A true cap on `payload_slots` (evict the lowest (epoch, round) at the cap); E+1 pending vertices only up to first_round(E+1)+LEAD (with B117) | FIXED (46594c5): an E+1 vertex is buffered only up to first_round(E+1)+LEAD, and `payload_slots` holds at most 2^16 slots (pruned by the floor, then cleared). Witness in `activation_checks_no_certificate_twice_and_none_a_buffered_vertex_carried` (an E+1 vertex past the lead is not buffered); mutant killed. The clear at the cap is a backstop with no witness (a forgotten slot only costs its checks again) |
| B119 | MEDIUM | B68 remainder: vertices buffered for E+1 carry their embedded certificates unchecked (PendingEpoch returns before E4) and activation re-evaluates them all in one uncounted call: each Byzantine member of C_{E+1} buffers 16 vertices with up to n well-formed junk certificates (every signer bit, the right committee hash), ~5n^2 failed pairings at every epoch boundary under the consensus lock (seconds at n~30 on a Pi, minutes at n=100). Activation also re-verifies every early certificate already verified on arrival | `ingress_v4.rs` 381-384; `v4.rs` 748-750, 919-929; `staging.rs` 22; `epoch.rs` 594-602 (round-5 consensus review) | Strip the embedded certificates before buffering a PendingEpoch vertex (E+1 certificates arrive as early certificates or by pull); ingest early certificates at activation without verifying them again | FIXED (46594c5): a vertex buffered for E+1 keeps none of its embedded certificates, and activation ingests the early certificates without verifying them again (they were verified under C_{E+1} on arrival). Witness `activation_checks_no_certificate_twice_and_none_a_buffered_vertex_carried` (activating verifies no certificate at all); two mutants killed |
| B120 | MEDIUM | B101 class: a replayed certificate at or below the GC floor is verified (a pairing; valid, so uncounted), written with a synced put, indexed, and its body requested again: a member replays thousands of GC'd certificates after every GC (~6.65 s) | `v4.rs` 936-957, 973-975, 1036-1043; `gc.rs` 151 (round-5 consensus review) | Drop a certificate whose round is at or below the GC floor before verifying it (IN-1 E3's rule for vertices) | FIXED (46594c5): a certificate at or below GC's cut (g - RETAIN_SLACK, where GC-3 forgets certificates) is dropped before it is verified. Between the cut and g certificates are still held, so a second digest for a held slot there is still verified and reported as a conflict: the first version dropped everything at or below g, which would have hidden such a conflict (its witness found the round-2 certificate still held above the cut). Witness `a_certificate_below_the_floor_is_not_verified`; mutant killed |
| B121 | MEDIUM | B108 regression: the fee-sweep key's due height is zero padded (`{due:020}`) but the classifier still takes `dec(h)`, which refuses a leading zero, so the key is unclassified and CL-1 refuses the write in production; `queue_fee_sweep` ignores the error and the share is lost (B83 reverted: a paid committee member without a CoinStore, e.g. a bootstrap operator, loses its fees). Not a fork. Missed because `StateDB::seeding()` bypasses CL-1 as well as WG-1, and both fee-sweep tests run under it | `executor` `sweep_key`; `class.rs` 285, 640; `transaction.rs` 183-193 (round-5 execution review) | `dec20` for the queue's height; the class: seeding relaxes WG-1 only, so an unclassified key fails every test that writes one | FIXED (46594c5): the fee-sweep queue's height is classified as 20 digits (`dec20`); the class: while a test seeds, a key under a state prefix that the classifier does not know is refused (seeding relaxes WG-1 and a fixture's own made-up keys only), so a state writer whose key format drifts fails its tests. Witnesses `seeding_refuses_an_unclassified_key_under_a_state_prefix`, `every_appendix_a_template_has_its_class`, the fee-sweep tests; three mutants killed |
| B122 | MEDIUM | B39/B53 incomplete: forwarding drops a transaction after 2 refusals and requeues at most 3 times, counted in members, not stake: two adjacent Byzantine members of a 7-member committee make an observer drop transactions its RPC answered `sent`; the forwarder awaits each member's answer in turn (10 s timeout), so a silent member caps an observer at ~25 tx/s and fills its mempool | `forward.rs` 177, 189-223, 252-265; `mempool` 77 (round-5 network review) | Drop only after members holding more than a third of the stake refused; requeue across at least f+1 distinct members; ask concurrently with a short timeout; strike members that stay silent | FIXED (46594c5): forwarding drops a transaction only once at least two members refused it and they hold more than a third of the current committee's stake (`PeerBook::stakes`); the groups of a pass are asked at once, and a member that did not answer is skipped for 30 s. Witnesses `a_byzantine_minority_cannot_drop_a_transaction_by_refusing` (seven members, two adjacent refusers), `a_silent_member_is_skipped_for_a_while`; two mutants killed |
| B123 | MEDIUM | SDK: `waitForConfirmation` polls `aincore_getTransaction`, which returns the mempool copy in the committed shape, and never reads the receipt: pending and aborted (charged) transactions are reported confirmed (a merchant accepts a transfer that aborted) | `aincore-js/src/connection.ts` 551-572; `api_local.rs` 1151-1166 (round-5 network review) | Poll the receipt; require a block height and `status == success`; mark pending results pending | FIXED (46594c5): the SDK's `waitForConfirmation` reads the receipt: `confirmed` only once a block executed the transaction successfully; it returns the status (`success`, `aborted`, `pending`, ...). Witness `aincore-js/test_sdk_inputs.ts` (`npm run test:inputs`); two mutants killed. The node's `aincore_getTransaction` shape is unchanged (other clients read it) |
| B124 | LOW | B52 remainder: a QC_VOTE reads and scans the whole held block before its duplicate checks: junk votes for any held height cost a block read under the consensus lock, uncounted | `dag.rs` 1752-1763; `qc_producer.rs` 1008-1020 (round-5 consensus review) | Check the round's QC and the signer's row first | FIXED (46594c5): a QC vote reads its block only if its signer is a member of the epoch it names, its round has no QC and the signer's vote is not held (`qc_producer::vote_still_needed`, point reads). | B125 | LOW | A member that lost its database (RC-3 abstain-then-resume) restarts its pull request numbers at 1, and every server that has not restarted refuses them until they pass the old highest: it can recover missing certificates and bodies only from pushes | `v4/pull.rs` 150-152, 396-416, 444-473 (round-5 consensus review) | Request numbers from the clock (milliseconds and a counter) | FIXED (46594c5): pull request numbers start past the clock (2^30 a second, `seq_floor`) as well as past the durable reservation, so a member that lost its database starts above every number it used. | B126 | LOW | Remaining uncounted work: a signer already recorded as an equivocator still costs a pairing for each fresh attestation of another digest; a vertex waiting for parent certificates keeps its valid embedded certificates without ingesting them, so each copy verifies them again | `vcert.rs` 467-490; `v4.rs` 725-747; `ingress_v4.rs` 411-434 (round-5 consensus review) | Stop verifying a signer once its equivocation is recorded; ingest verified embedded certificates on PendingCert | FIXED (46594c5): a signer recorded as an equivocator is not verified again; a waiting vertex's embedded certificates that verify are ingested at once and the buffered vertex keeps none. ## Round 6: the audit of 2026-10-06 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-5 tree)

Consensus and sync, execution and economics, network and RPC, and an adversarial review of round 5's fixes. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a way to frame an honest member. Both HIGHs are gaps in round-5 fixes (B132 of B116, B133 of B122); B149 is a contract choice, settled here.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B132 | HIGH | B116 incomplete: the 16 kept handshake slots are one shared pool, first come, and a kept IP skips every other bound but the 4 per IP: f Byzantine members hold them all (n=13, f=4: 4 x 4), an outside flood of 28 IPs over 14 /16 groups holds the other 112, and a restarting honest member is refused both ways (n=3f+1: a halt). DNS bootnodes (dialable since B130, documented for mainnet) and reserved peers (PeerIds, unknown before the handshake) give no kept IP at all, so a fresh launch with the documented flags has none | `sessions.rs` 826, 893-906, 964-1001; `p2p.rs` 191, 202-205, 718-733, 764 (round-6 adversarial and network reviews) | Each kept IP its own slots outside the shared bound (the total pending cap grows by them); resolve boot host names at boot and every minute; record a member's IP from the connection; reserved peers given as addresses | OPEN |
| B133 | HIGH | B122 regression: any failed ask benches a member for 30 s, and a busy answer (both TX_SUBMIT permits held, or the byte budget spent) looks like silence: junk TX_SUBMIT batches, or the observer's own catch-up, get every honest member benched; with them benched one Byzantine member refusing everything is the only one asked, and `return_refused` drops a transaction after 3 passes (~0.75 s) while the in-pass stake rule never applies; the RPC already said "sent" | `forward.rs` 224-234, 264-274, 313-318; `mempool` 771-790; `main.rs` 815-848; `p2p.rs` 472, 582-590 (round-6 network and adversarial reviews) | An explicit busy answer, never a strike; a strike only on a timeout; one ask in flight per member; refusals carried across passes and weighed by stake (drop only past a third); a pass with busy or skipped members is no attempt | OPEN |

## Round 7: the audit of 2026-10-06 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-6 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 6's fixes. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a way to frame an honest member. One CRITICAL, found by two reviewers apart: a halt any funded account can cause with one transaction (B150). Most of the rest are gaps in round-6 fixes (B151 and B154 of B133/B134, B152 and B163 of B132, B158 and B169 of B148, B159 of B145, B160 of B146, B161 of B149, B162 of B140, B165 of B147) and one of B124 (B164).

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B150 | CRITICAL | A published module (or struct) named `<SELF>` halts every validator for good: Move's identifier check accepts `<SELF>` (the reference `empty_module()` is named so) and nothing in admission, ingress or the bytecode verifier checks names, while CL-1's `ident` refuses it. The publish writes `module_{sender}_<SELF>` (or a resource key ending `::<SELF>`), the block's write gate refuses the whole batch, the executor panics on the refused batch and `abort_on_panic` aborts the process; every node re-executes the committed block on restart and aborts again. Cost: one transaction's gas | `class.rs` 82-87, 228-232; `transaction.rs` 194-203; executor `lib.rs` 3844-3849; `node/src/lib.rs` 22-38; move-core `identifier.rs` 63-71 (round-7 execution and network reviews, apart) | Classify every key of a transaction's write set before it is absorbed: a key no class names aborts that transaction, charged. Not refused at admission: that would deserialize modules on the vertex ingress path; the charged abort pays for the block space it took | OPEN |
| B151 | MEDIUM | B122/B133 incomplete: one slow member caps every observer at about 32 tx/s. A member is struck only on the 8 s timeout; one answering after ~7.9 s (busy, or accept-and-drop) never is, and each pass waited for its slowest batch and for the pass before it, a pass loaning at most 256 transactions: the observer's mempool fills and its RPC refuses new transactions | `forward.rs` 52, 378, 421-434 (round-7 network review) | Passes run apart (a bounded number at once); a member has one ask in flight at most, so a slow one holds only the batch it was asked | OPEN |
| B152 | MEDIUM | B132 incomplete: (a) a Byzantine member's identify reports rotate its kept IPs; each new pair skips the per-group and per-IP bounds, and the handshakes it opened from the IPs rotated out stay pending for the 10 s timeout: ~1,308 IPs fill `MAX_PENDING_TOTAL` and every inbound connection is refused, honest members' kept IPs too (at n = 4 a restarted node cannot rejoin, as in B116). (b) The two most recent IPs from any source are kept, so a member reporting two routable interfaces pushes out the IP it connects from | `sessions.rs` 901-913, 958-974, 1139-1181; `p2p.rs` 932-938 (round-7 network and adversarial reviews) | One slot for the IP the member's connection comes from, one for a reported IP; each replaced at most once a handshake timeout | OPEN |
| B153 | LOW | B10 class, missed instance: `aincore_getToken`, `aincore_getTokens` and `aincore_getTokenBalance` read the dead `token:` and `token_balance:` keys and always answer null, `[]` or "0" (the SDK passes that on). Same root: the executor's conflict tokens for token_factory still name the dead `TokenRegistry@0x1` and not the rows B135 writes (harmless while every token_factory call also names the staking keys) | `api_local.rs` 1496-1532, 1646-1666; executor `analyze_tx` (round-7 network and execution reviews) | Read the Move state (`CreatorTokens`, `TokenWallet`); name the rows token_factory writes as its conflict tokens | OPEN |
| B154 | MEDIUM | B134/B133 incomplete: a Byzantine minority still censors transactions observers forward. Records are never forgotten when a transaction settles, so under load (more than ~16.7 new transactions a second) the full table evicts the longest-pending live record, whose TTL and members restart; and a re-forward asks one member per 30 s, so the 600 s TTL tries about 20 members while f can reach 85 (adjacency in PeerId order costs nothing to grind) | `forward.rs` 56-61, 148-170, 329-346 (round-7 adversarial review) | Forget a record no pass loaned for two retry periods (a settled or dropped transaction), evict the least recently loaned; a re-forward goes on to untried members until those that took the transaction hold more than a third of the stake | OPEN |
| B155 | MEDIUM | B117/B136 class: a twin that cannot be staged is staged again on every copy: a copy whose payload memo passed skips the payload check and gets `Stage`, `stage_and_attest` runs again, and an `EvidenceOnly` outcome is not remembered, so no early exit catches the next copy. Each costs a parse of the slot's other body (up to 768 KiB), a clone and hash for the twin evidence, and a pairing for the stored guard, uncounted, for the rest of the epoch (also after any restart, through a third twin of a full slot) | `v4.rs` 801-805, 903-958; `ingress_v4.rs` 345-366; `vcert.rs` 289-302; `v4/evidence.rs` 58-88; `staging.rs` 140-173 (round-7 consensus review) | Remember a staged body that did not stage, per slot (bounded), and drop its copies before Layer S unless this node now holds its certificate; attest only a slot not self-attested to another digest; build the twin evidence only when its row is absent | OPEN |
| B156 | LOW | B133 regression: `TX_BUSY` has no back-off: the next pass, 250 ms later, asks the busy member again, every ask is charged to the observer's sync budget at the member, and the over-budget asks come back busy too, so the loop sustains itself and the observer's block sync is refused | `forward.rs` 430-434; `p2p.rs` 614; `main.rs` 842-848 (round-7 adversarial review) | A member that said it is busy rests a second; transaction submits on a budget of their own (B157) | OPEN |
| B157 | LOW | A `TX_SUBMIT` batch costs its sender one token of the shared sync budget while it costs the member up to 64 signature checks: ~16 hosts sending signed junk keep observers no member reserved at `TX_BUSY` on every member until their transactions' TTL | `main.rs` 815-848; `p2p.rs` 614 (round-7 network review) | A submit budget of its own, charged a token per transaction (rate: an observer's full mempool per re-forward period) | OPEN |
| B158 | LOW | B148 incomplete: only copies of pending or refused bodies skip Layer S; copies that end `Stale` or `Drop` (an old vertex at or below g replayed, payload beyond the cursor's lead, a closed epoch) run the parse, two serializations, the hash and the signature check every time, uncharged, as do hash and signature failures themselves | `ingress_v4.rs` 432-488; `v4.rs` 712-733 (round-7 consensus and adversarial reviews) | Run the filters that need no authentication before Layer S; charge a failed hash or signature check (B51: no honest node sends one) | OPEN |

| B161 | LOW | B149 incomplete on the raise path: a certificate conflict for an earlier epoch moves the offense's scope back but keeps the later offense's split, so a pool that grew between them has its later deposits burned and one that shrank is cut too little | `delegation.move` 568-572, 719-727 (round-7 adversarial review) | On a raise to an earlier epoch take that epoch's split (self and delegated weight) with it | OPEN |
| B162 | LOW | B140's class not closed (preventive): the offense-record writes still discard their errors (`let _ =`), and `commit_kv_updates` in epoch advance, reward payout and settlement only logs a refused batch (a partial epoch advance); every key involved classifies today | executor `lib.rs` 2322-2433, 4614 (round-7 execution and adversarial reviews) | Through `system_put` and `refuse_block`: a refused write fails the block | OPEN |

| B165 | LOW | B147 incomplete: the free-copy shortcut compares only with the first attestation seen: a member sends a committee-hash variant first, then its genuine attestation, and every replay of the genuine one costs a pairing | `vcert.rs` 492-503 (round-7 consensus review) | An attestation identical to one counted is a duplicate before any check | OPEN |

## Round 8: the audit of 2026-10-06 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-7 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 7's fixes. No CRITICAL. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a way to frame an honest member. One HIGH, a regression of a round-6 fix (B170 of B139); the rest are gaps in round-7 fixes (B171 of B158, B172 and B173 of B157, B175 of B167, B178 of B159, B179 of B162, B180 of B166), one missed instance each of B94 (B174), B59 (B176) and B153 (B177).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B170 | HIGH | B139 regression: the block's VM cache was flushed after every batch that republished a module, and move-vm flushes all of it (the stdlib too), so each upgrade made the rest of the block load and verify again what it had loaded, uncharged (every prologue reloads `coin`'s closure, ~33 KB in 17 modules; a republish's compatibility check also loads the replaced version's whole closure, never paid for). A few accounts alternating cheap upgrades (~300-475 a block at the target gas, ~0.27 AIN a block) add tens of MB of verification to every block on every node | executor `lib.rs` 3856-3858, 5135-5143, 5618-5623; `deps.rs` 164-177; move-vm `runtime.rs` 122-123, 196-200, `loader.rs` 559-567 (round-8 execution review) | No flush mid-block: an upgrade runs from the next block (each block has its own VM). | B171 | LOW | B158 incomplete: a copy of the previous epoch's vertex (a replay, or one a member of that committee signed anew at any round) ran Layer S and was dropped, uncharged and unremembered, for the whole of the active epoch; a next-epoch vertex while this node does not hold that epoch's record went through `signed_by_member`, whose hash and signature failures were not charged, and each copy that showed no new gap was authenticated again | `ingress_v4.rs` 132-145, 447-459, 487-496; `v4.rs` 733-771, 886 (round-8 consensus review) | Drop the closed epoch's copies before Layer S (nothing reads the verdict); authenticate a next-epoch copy only when it shows a gap not wanted yet; charge `signed_by_member`'s hash failures, and its signature failures when the author is a staked member of the active committee (a member's key derives its address) | OPEN |
| B172 | MEDIUM | B157 regression: `SubmitBudget` counted a batch's transactions by parsing the whole request (up to 256 KiB, a string per element) on the swarm task, the loop that carries every consensus push, before any token was spent; a batch costing more than the burst (e.g. ~65,000 empty strings) was refused without being charged: a free full parse per request | `p2p.rs` 617-622; `sessions.rs` 822-825; `forward.rs` 303-307 (round-8 network and adversarial reviews) | Price a batch by its length, read unparsed: a quota in bytes, `TX_SUBMIT_PER_SEC` transactions of the smallest size a member checks the signature of (`MIN_TX_WIRE`, 408 bytes), so at most that many checks a second; burst four requests at the cap | OPEN |

## Round 9: the audit of 2026-10-06 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-8 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 8's fixes. No CRITICAL. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a way to frame an honest member. One HIGH (B188, a gap in B70/B94/B98) and two MEDIUM (B185, B189); four gaps in round-8 fixes (B181 of B171, B182 of B175, B183 of B172, B184 of B173) and four new LOWs. Fixing them found a regression in round 7's B154 (its status says how).

## Round 11: the audit of 2026-10-07 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-10 tree)

Execution and economics (with the pinned move-vm runtime's limits against Aptos production, carried from round 10), network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 10's fixes. No CRITICAL, no HIGH. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a panic reachable from peer, RPC, sync or restore input. Four MEDIUM (B211, B215, B219, B220). Two are regressions of round-10 fixes (B220 of B197, B225 of B205) and six are gaps in them (B215 of B206, B216 of B201, B217 and B219 of B197, B218 of B195, B224 of B202 and B204, which also showed B169's acceptance resting on a false premise). B226 was found while fixing B224.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B211 | MEDIUM | The Move VM ran without its runtime type checks (`paranoid_type_checks` false; Aptos production sets it): the interpreter keeps no type stack and trusts the bytecode verifier alone, and a type confusion the verifier missed would panic outside move's `catch_unwind`, which B92's abort-on-panic turns into every re-executing validator aborting: a permanent halt (B150 class). No verifier bypass was exhibited in aptos-v1.3.0 | `vm_move/src/lib.rs` 225-242; move-vm `interpreter.rs` 72-460 (round-11 execution review) | `paranoid_type_checks: true` | OPEN |

B169 re-decided below | OPEN |
| B225 | LOW | B205 regression and B190 incomplete: a committee parse per message, uncharged: B205 put the committee before the held block, so a member's QC naming a pruned or future height parsed the epoch record (~130 KB at 256 members) and sorted and hashed it; a QC vote under any fresh signer name passed the point reads and parsed it too | `qc_producer.rs` 922-938, 963-979; `dag.rs` 697-716, 1805 (round-11 consensus review) | A height not held is known from small rows (the latest height, the prune cursor, the kept pins) before the committee; an epoch's committee is parsed once per record, with its hash; a vote in a name outside the committee is charged | OPEN |
| B226 | LOW | One vertex copy carrying a junk certificate on each ref cost a pairing per ref (up to 256, ~0.5 s on a Pi) before the failures, counted after the message, muted its sender: a burst per Byzantine member each mute period | `ingress_v4.rs` (E4's loop) (found while fixing B224) | Once an embedded certificate fails, the copy's others are not checked | OPEN |

B169 re-decided (with B224): a body now reaches Layer S at most twice per slot (an author's third digest is dropped before it unless certified here), and no copy of a digest it has judged runs it again (held pending, refused, evicted while it would be evicted again, dropped this tick, early, unstageable); transport padding is refused before it, charged. A second digest is evidence whenever the first body is still held, staged or pending; a first body dropped after Layer S (early, past the lead, refused) leaves none, though its slot still takes no third check. What remains is one authentication per distinct body: one a slot for an honest member. ACCEPTED on that premise; a tighter byte rate would still delay honest catch-up.

## Round 12: the audit of 2026-10-07 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-11 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 11's fixes. No CRITICAL. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a panic reachable from peer, RPC, sync or restore input; the adversarial review of round 11 found nothing above LOW. One HIGH (B231) and one MEDIUM (B232), both regressions of round-11 fixes (B215 and B216), found by the network review; the rest LOW, four of them gaps in round-11 fixes (B227 of B213, B229 of B224, B234 and B235 of B219/B220).

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## Round 13: the audit of 2026-10-07 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-12 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 12's fixes. No CRITICAL. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a panic reachable from peer, RPC, sync or restore input; the execution review found nothing above LOW. Two HIGH: B239 (found independently by the network and the adversarial reviews: B231's class still open) and B248 (consensus: certificate pulls blockable by any member, a halt with f members); one MEDIUM, B240 (B234 reopened B182); the rest LOW, five of them gaps in round-12 fixes (B241, B242, B244, B246, B247). B249 was found by the rounds 6-12 verification run, not by a reviewer.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B239 | HIGH | B231's class still open: the pool's maps were keyed by each transaction's text, so a replacement's lookup walked the queue hashing every transaction ahead of the one it replaced, and a full pool's eviction scan hashed the whole queue on every refused admission (up to `MAX_PENDING_BYTES`, 8 MiB, under the mempool lock); both refusals were free and repeatable, and `serve_tx_submit` held the lock across a 64-transaction batch while the proposer waits on it under the consensus lock | `mempool/src/lib.rs` 144, 325-334, 456-482, 1036-1096; `forward.rs` 339-360; `dag.rs` 1397 (round-13 network and adversarial reviews, independently) | Every map and the queue keyed by a transaction id (the text hashed once, at admission); the transaction a replacement replaces found by its sender and sequence number; the eviction scan remembered until the pool, the committed height or the base fee changes; the pool's count read from its index; `serve_tx_submit` takes the lock one transaction at a time | OPEN |
| B240 | MEDIUM | B234 reopened B182: the stored-certificate RPCs (`getQuorumCert` and its aliases) waited in the dry runs' queue, so with the queue long (about ten IPs running maximum-gas estimates) the bridge's certificate call failed without the certificate and the bridge read it as "not final" every poll | `api_local.rs` 810-816, 1289-1303, 2515-2548; bridge `aincore_client.rs` 373-382, 434-440 (round-13 adversarial and network reviews) | The stored-certificate calls leave the queue: a remembered outcome is answered at once, an unremembered one verified only on a dry permit free now (never waiting, never a slot the queue counts on), else the certificate is answered unverified (B182); `verifyQuorumCertificate` stays queued | OPEN |
| B241 | LOW | B235's class, missed: `/get_network_info` read two whole blocks (for their timestamps) and 21 transaction lists on the actix worker each call, on no slot and no lease, 100 calls a second an IP; the SDK's `getNetworkInfo` (the wallets' chain id, B223) calls it | `api_local.rs` 2730-2775, 2805-2818 (round-13 network review) | The recent TPS computed once a height and remembered | OPEN |
| B242 | LOW | B233's indexer batch was all-or-nothing (500 blocks): one receipt failing its three tries threw away every receipt read and fetched the batch again, so recurring bursts of other clients' heavy reads (over ~5 s) stopped the height advancing; and each receipt opened a new HTTP client | `indexer/src/main.rs` 16, 564-580, 749-759 (round-13 network review) | Receipts read in block order until the first that cannot be read: the blocks before its block are written and the height advances to the last of them, its block read again next loop; one HTTP client for every call | OPEN |

| B246 | LOW | B227's class: the memory quota credited values it never held (scalars, references, entry arguments, `move_from` results): `LdU64; Pop` credited 16 units for 2 gas, so the counter could be kept near zero while copies stayed live, and live memory was bounded by gas (about ten times `MEMORY_QUOTA` at `MAX_GAS_LIMIT`, estimated) rather than by the quota | `vm_move/src/gas.rs` 247-251, 302-310, 373-382 (round-13 adversarial review) | Holds and credits measured by heap size (a container's legacy size; nothing for a scalar, an address or a reference): a pack, a vector pack or push, a `move_from` and a native's result held, their reverse credited as they were held; gas still prices every value by its size | OPEN |

| B249 | LOW | B229 regression: a body past the lead with uncertified parents and a round outside its record's range (over `ABSOLUTE_ROUND_CEILING`, or below the first round) was dropped before Layer S (`Drop`), so a verdict every node gives (`Invalid`, charged) became a context's | `ingress_v4.rs` 569-599 (the rounds 6-12 verification run: `layer_s_refuses_what_every_node_refuses` failed) | The record's round range is checked first, before the lead filters, as Layer S checks it | OPEN |

Not ledgered: the execution review's notes below LOW (the gas schedule is a fixed default, so tuning it needs a fresh genesis; the memory quota's under-count was an implicit invariant, now explicit in B246's heap rule).

## Round 14: the audit of 2026-10-07 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-13 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 13's fixes. No CRITICAL. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a panic reachable from peer, RPC, sync or restore input; the adversarial review of round 13 and the execution review found nothing above LOW. Two HIGH, both liveness under f Byzantine members, from the consensus and sync review: B255 (a member cut off past the GC window could never rebuild what it missed, so with f members withholding their votes finality stopped for good) and B256 (one member held every catching-up node's block sync at about a block a minute). One MEDIUM, B253 (one host's many addresses starved every heavy RPC call). The rest LOW; B250 and B251 were each found by two reviewers independently.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B250 | LOW | B248 made the certificate answer budget per (slot, member asked), so a want that is never satisfied (a stuck node's gap, up to 256 rounds of n authors) kept an entry for every member asked, n − 1 per slot: about 0.5 GB at n = 100 after some hours, 3.3 GB at n = 256 | `v4/pull.rs` 383-389, 397-399, 676-679; `v4.rs` 276 (round-14 adversarial and consensus reviews, independently) | A wanted slot keeps the turns of the last `MAX_ASKED_PER_SLOT` (4) members asked only; an answer later than four more asks of its slot is dropped and the slot asked again | OPEN |
| B251 | LOW | B247's member ids were given for good: after `MAX_MEMBER_IDS` (1,024) members over a forwarding node's life (committee rotation, re-keyed stakers) a new member got none, its verdicts were recorded nowhere, an accept-and-drop member without an id was asked first at every re-forward (B53/B134/B154 reopened for its senders) and its refusals counted toward no drop | `forward.rs` 75-77, 160-176, 281-299 (round-14 adversarial and network reviews, independently) | The records and the ids their sets use under one lock: with every id given, those no live record names are freed first (an id still named is never given again) | OPEN |
| B252 | LOW | B246's quota credited a container field of a resource loaded from storage when it was overwritten through a reference, though loading held nothing, so a transaction could keep the counter below what it held (about half as much again as `MEMORY_QUOTA` within `MAX_GAS_LIMIT`, estimated; deterministic) | `vm_move/src/gas.rs` 236-246, 323-331, 432-440 (round-14 execution review) | A resource loaded from storage is held (as Aptos's memory tracker holds it); an entry function's arguments, credited once each though never held, are accepted: at most the transaction's 100 KiB, 1% of the quota | OPEN |
| B253 | MEDIUM | B217/B220 incomplete: a client is an IPv4 address or an IPv6 /64, so one host with a /56 (256 clients) or a /48 (65,536) kept both slots of each heavy pool taken with long calls (an estimate to `MAX_GAS_LIMIT`, an address in no block), and every other client's heavy calls (receipts, the bridge's blocks, estimates, DA samples, REST blocks) were busy; and a receipt parsed its whole block for its hash, on a read slot | `api_local.rs` 848-874, 2023-2055, 2521-2556, 2601-2627, 2947-2969 (round-14 network review) | Each pool behind a gate per network group (IPv4 /24, IPv6 /48): a group runs one call of a pool at a time; a receipt reads its block's hash from the block's certificate row (about 2 KB) when held, else from the header alone | OPEN |

| B255 | HIGH | B248's halt still reachable past the retention window: g (anchor − GC_DEPTH) took no input from finality, and GC deleted rounds at or below g − RETAIN_SLACK from disk and memory, so an honest member cut off for over 100 rounds (a restart, an upgrade, a partition before GST) found every server answering `unknown` for the rounds it needed, could build and vote on nothing, and block sync and adoption need QCs; with f members withholding their (unslashable) QC votes no QC formed without it, finality stopped for good and the chain halted at the next epoch boundary, released at the Byzantine members' will | `ordering.rs` 1099; `v4/gc.rs` 40, 47-55, 140-154; `v4/pull.rs` 569-615; `sync/src/lib.rs` 1075-1081; `dag.rs` 528-534, 2001-2010; contract LA-6 (round-14 consensus review) | The rounds above the anchor of a member's lowest block without a QC less GC_DEPTH + RETAIN_SLACK are kept and served (GC-3's cut, ingress's B120 cut, boot and the closed epoch's tail), g itself unchanged; a member proposes nothing with `MAX_UNFINALIZED_BLOCKS` (50) blocks above that block, which bounds what is kept to twice the GC window | OPEN |
| B256 | HIGH | B33 incomplete: block sync ordered sessions by their unauthenticated advertised height and synced each to completion, striking a session only when it brought nothing, so one member advertising a huge height and serving one real block (with its QC) a batch just inside the 60 s request timeout held every pass of every catching-up node at about a block a minute while the chain made about nine: observers stalled for good, and a member offline past the DAG window never rejoined (B255) | `sync/src/lib.rs` 702-708, 709-730, 757-832; `main.rs` 1144-1154 (round-14 consensus review) | A session's turn of a pass is bounded (`SYNC_TURN`, 60 s, each request within what is left); a turn that runs out below the session's height having brought less than a full batch (500 blocks or 8 MiB of transactions) is slow, struck as an empty one is, and a struck session's strike is cleared only by a turn that is not slow | OPEN |

| B259 | LOW | B239's per-item locking did not bound the proposer's wait: `std::sync::Mutex` is not fair, the submit loop took the lock again ~100 ns after giving it back, so the proposal, waiting under the consensus write lock, usually waited for the whole 64-transaction batch (milliseconds, each admission now cheap) | `forward.rs` 408-421; `dag.rs` 1397 (round-14 network review; runtime behaviour of the lock unverified) | The consensus thread's waits for the pool's lock are counted (`lock_for_consensus`, every consensus-side lock), and a submitter lets a waiting consensus thread go first before each admission (`yield_to_consensus`, the submit batch and the RPC) | OPEN |

## Round 15: the audit of 2026-10-07 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-14 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 14's fixes. No CRITICAL. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a panic reachable from peer, RPC, sync or restore input. The execution review found nothing above LOW and no new LOW. Two HIGH, each found by two reviewers independently, both incomplete round-14 liveness fixes: B262 (B255's kept rounds started one anchor too high and were bounded in blocks, not rounds) and B263 (B256's strikes were cleared by ending a turn early, and sessions at this node's own height outranked struck members). Two MEDIUM: B264 (no node could block-sync past a block larger than a sync answer's frame) and B265 (one client stalled the indexer's API and its indexing). The rest LOW.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B262 | HIGH | B255 incomplete: a block's committed sequence walks down to the floor in force before its anchor (the previous anchor less GC_DEPTH), but the kept rounds started at the block's own anchor less GC_DEPTH, so after a run of skipped anchors (f members attesting everything but the leaders' vertices while one honest member is away, or asynchrony before GST) a member rebuilding the block needed rounds every honest peer had deleted, could not vote, and with f members withholding QC votes the chain stopped proposing for good 50 blocks later; and the kept window was bounded in blocks, not rounds, so f members letting an anchor commit every ~200 rounds kept every round in every honest member's memory (about 1.2 GiB an hour at f = 2, reloaded at boot) | `v4.rs` 680-693, 198; `v4/gc.rs` 41; `v4/epoch.rs` 684-687; `ordering.rs` 1036-1043, 1099; contract C1 (round-15 consensus and adversarial reviews, independently) | A block's pending QC row records the floor its decision walked to (`decision_floor`, the engine's g before the anchor), and the kept rounds start there less RETAIN_SLACK (GC-3, ingress, boot, the closed epoch's tail); a member also proposes nothing while that floor is below g and its round is `MAX_KEPT_ROUNDS` (GC_DEPTH + RETAIN_SLACK + LEAD, 300) above it, and meanwhile rebroadcasts its latest vertex every 4 ticks so a member that rejoins learns the gap; the "twice the GC window" bound is dropped (rounds per block are unbounded) | OPEN |
| B263 | HIGH | B256 incomplete: only a turn the clock ended could be slow, so a session that brought one real block slowly and then answered empty at once cleared its strike and kept the first turn of every pass; a full batch was measured in transaction bytes while the server cuts at 8 MiB of block JSON, so a slow client's honest servers were struck; and sessions advertising exactly this node's height took block turns, were never struck and outranked every struck member (members were struck whenever their newest block had no QC yet), strikes never expired, so three Sybil peers ended an observer's block sync for good | `sync/src/lib.rs` 705-721, 735-742, 786-808, 811-847, 850-855, 862-868, 1119-1125 (round-15 consensus and adversarial reviews, independently) | A turn is judged however it ends (`Turn::struck`): it strikes when an answer took over `SLOW_ANSWER` (20 s) with less than a full batch, or when it ended short of the session's height without a full batch, counted in the server's unit (encoded block bytes, half its cut); a turn that reached the session's height, or stopped at a block offered without its QC (its server's newest), ends well; strikes expire after `STRIKE_TTL` (600 s); only sessions above this node's height take block turns (up to three at its height are asked for finality only, at once); turns go to members first, then the fewest live strikes, then the most blocks brought last turn | OPEN |

## Round 16: the audit of 2026-10-07 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-15 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 15's fixes. No CRITICAL. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, nor a panic reachable from peer, RPC, sync or restore input. Three HIGH: B272 (a transaction carried twice made a certified block no node could import), and two incomplete round-15 fixes, each found by two reviewers independently: B273 (B262's floor was read only at ticks, so the first message after a block's commit cut with a stale one) and B274 (B263's turn order still followed claims and reset on reconnect). One MEDIUM, found by three reviewers: B275 (B264's parted fetch did not work under the server's answer budget, and started on an unverified QC). The rest LOW.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B272 | HIGH | A transaction carried twice (in two vertices of one anchor's history, as a Byzantine member can arrange for its own account at any anchor it leads) ran at a later copy when its first could not run (its sequence number, its payer's balance), and the body put it at the first copy's place, so the body re-executed in its own order reached another state root: no observer, RPC node, bridge, indexer or member needing sync could import the certified block, at the cost of two transactions' gas | `dag.rs` 1075; `core/executor/src/lib.rs` 1729-1745, 3839, 4133; `sync/src/lib.rs` 306-345 (round-16 execution review) | Each raw transaction is tried at its first copy only; later copies are dropped before scheduling, so the body is what ran, in the order it ran | OPEN |
| B273 | HIGH | B262 incomplete: `finality_floor` was read at boot and at each tick only, but GC cuts after every message once g rises; a block's pending row is written as its anchor raises g, so the next message (f members can send one at will) cut at g − RETAIN_SLACK with the last tick's floor (none: u64::MAX) and deleted, on every honest member, the rounds that block's decision walked; a member coming back could not rebuild it, and with f members withholding votes the chain stopped for good | `v4.rs` 562, 685-705, 776-778, 1657; `v4/gc.rs` 10-41; `dag.rs` 805-816, 1247, 1263 (round-16 consensus and adversarial reviews, independently) | The floor is read again whenever g has moved since it was last read, before any cut (GC, the ingress cuts, boot) | OPEN |
| B274 | HIGH | B263 incomplete: block turns were ordered, among sessions that brought nothing, by the height a session claims; a session never asked ranked first, and its standing was dropped with its session, so a reconnect reset a struck one; and a turn ended well on the server's word (its turn-time height at or below this node's, or any block offered without a QC), so three Byzantine members claiming the most took every turn of every pass, for good: observers stopped, and a member past the DAG window could not catch up while f members withheld votes | `sync/src/lib.rs` 878-888, 901-918, 1006-1011, 1092-1096, 1110-1122 (round-16 consensus and adversarial reviews, independently) | Turns ordered by standing only: members, strikes still counting, blocks brought (unknown = 0), then the session that waited longest for a turn; standings (strikes, yield, last turn) outlive reconnects for `STRIKE_TTL`, at most 1,024 sessions; a turn that finds this node at the session's height ends well only if this node reached the height the session claimed for the pass, and leaves its yield; a block offered without its QC ends a turn well only within `MAX_UNFINALIZED_BLOCKS` of the height claimed | OPEN |
| B275 | MEDIUM | B264 incomplete: the server's answer budget (a token each 64 KiB, 12.8 a second) dropped the next part request after a part (8 MiB of hex) with no busy reply, so no block over a batch could be fetched from an honest server; the fetch started on any QC naming the height (unverified), and ran outside the turn for up to ~9.5 minutes, holding 512 MiB; the server read a whole block for any part request and for each request beside an oversized block; and a body padded in its anchor hash cost a full Reed-Solomon encode before the cheap checks | `sync/src/lib.rs` 1006-1013, 1145-1190, 1703-1722, 1761-1774, 525-528; `core/node/src/p2p.rs` 493-526, 639-661; `sessions.rs` 989-994 (round-16 adversarial, consensus and network reviews) | The oversized answer carries the block's header, checked against a QC that verifies under its committee (`verify_header_qc`) before any part; parts are the block's stored text (not hex), a busy answer is sent and waited out, the fetch stays within the turn and resumes at the session's next turn from where it stopped; the whole must be the server's stored text (padding refused, its server struck); a server serves parts only of blocks a batch answer named, from a bounded index (no read for any other request); the anchor binding is checked before the roots | OPEN |
| B276 | LOW | The settlement cap (32 a call) could prune a settled offense while a correlated one within D was still due, so the latter settled at the next call with Q missing that weight: under-slashed (the fraction is quadratic) | `delegation.move` 718, 749 (round-16 execution review) | A call the cap stopped prunes nothing | OPEN |
| B277 | LOW | Receipts, hashed into `receipts_root` (which QCs bind), were written with `json!`: their key order followed serde_json's map, which a `preserve_order` feature any crate in a build can enable turns to insertion order, so two builds would compute different roots (latent: nothing enables it today) | `core/executor/src/lib.rs` 1279-1284, 1492 (round-16 execution review) | Receipt text sorts its keys at every depth whatever the map keeps (the bytes the sorted map wrote) | OPEN |

| B279 | LOW | The indexer charged each request to its peer's network group only, so behind a reverse proxy every client was the proxy's group: one query at a time for the whole public | `indexer/src/main.rs` 1040-1052 (round-16 network review) | Trusted proxies and their client-address header (`AINCORE_INDEXER_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, `AINCORE_INDEXER_CLIENT_IP_HEADER`), by the node's rule: the last element of the header's last line, canonical peers | OPEN |

## Round 17: the audit of 2026-10-07 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-16 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 16's fixes. One CRITICAL: B282 (a member back past the GC window signed a second body for its first slot, a twin any Byzantine member could carry as evidence: an honest member slashed in full and jailed). One HIGH: B283 (B262 incomplete: the newest vertex a waiting member sent again was past the lead of a member coming back, so it never learned the gap). Three MEDIUM, each found by two reviewers independently, each an incomplete round-16 fix: B284 (B272's first-copy rule let one member censor a sender's queue for free), B285 (B275's single parted fetch: a block served by two sessions never arrived) and B286 (B274's order still let fresh identities take every turn). The rest LOW. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B282 | CRITICAL | A member back past the GC window signed a second body for its first slot. GC-3 deleted its producer guards (`vproposed`) and attestation guards at or below the cut and forgot its certificates; `current_round` fell back to the epoch's first round while no quorum round above the cut was held; nothing checked the round against g. A Byzantine member that kept the first body carried the twin as `SLASH_EVIDENCE:`: the honest member was slashed in full and jailed, and repeated, its seats went to the Byzantine members | `v4/gc.rs` 62-70, 107-118, 150-154; `v4.rs` 1652-1661, 1703-1738, 1842-1898; executor `verify_v4_twins` 4378-4440 (round-17 consensus review) | A member proposes only above g and above the highest round it proposed in the epoch, kept in a row GC-3 deletes only with the whole epoch (`consensus:vproposed_high`), checked at the tick and again in `propose`'s transaction | OPEN |
| B283 | HIGH | B262 incomplete: `MAX_KEPT_ROUNDS` (`GC_DEPTH + RETAIN_SLACK + LEAD`, 300) let a waiting member's newest vertex, which carries payload, sit past the lead of a member coming back (its cursor one past the anchor before the block without a QC: that block's floor plus `GC_DEPTH` plus 1). Ingress drops a payload past the lead before Layer S and wants nothing, so the member never learned the gap, never rebuilt the block, and with f members withholding votes no QC formed again | `v4.rs` 207-208, 723-730, 1695-1701, 1734-1738; `ingress_v4.rs` 583-593 (round-17 consensus review) | `MAX_KEPT_ROUNDS = GC_DEPTH + LEAD` (250): the newest vertex stays within that lead | OPEN |
| B284 | MEDIUM | B272's first-copy rule: a Byzantine member copied a sender's queued transactions into its own vertex ordered ahead of the honest copies (an earlier round, or a ground digest), where their sequence numbers could not run yet, at no cost (a copy that cannot pay pays nothing). The honest copies were then skipped as carried, stayed on loan, and after three loans were forgotten, `seen_txs` refusing the sender's resubmissions | executor `lib.rs` 3774-3779, 1763-1778, 4177; mempool `lib.rs` 65, 1052-1066, 1266-1296; `dag.rs` 1049-1077, 1405 (round-17 adversarial and execution reviews, independently) | Every copy is tried; a transaction runs at most once, at the first copy that can run, and stands in the body at the place of that copy (its place carried through the batches) | OPEN |
| B285 | MEDIUM | B275 incomplete: one parted fetch was kept, for one session. | B286 | MEDIUM | B274 incomplete: among equals a session never given a turn went first and ties went by PeerId, so three fresh identities a pass, PeerIds ground to sort low, took every turn from honest non-members whose standing had expired or not begun: a node with no member session above it (an observer, an RPC node, an indexer's node) never synced | `sync/src/lib.rs` 217-218, 1033-1059 (round-17 adversarial and consensus reviews, independently) | Equals go by how long they have waited for a turn: since their last turn or, never given one, since this node first saw them (a fresh identity starts at the back); ties by the PeerId hashed under a per-node salt; sessions at this node's height asked for finality in the same salted order | OPEN |

## Round 18: the audit of 2026-10-07 (four independent read-only reviews of the round-17 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, and an adversarial review of round 17's fixes. No CRITICAL; nothing above LOW in execution and economics. One HIGH, found by two reviewers independently: B300 (B262/B283 incomplete: a member coming back still never learned the gap, after skipped anchors, past its lead, or at an epoch boundary, so a block without its QC was never rebuilt and the chain halted). Two MEDIUM: B299 (answers refused at the held-answer bound were never charged, and every node read whole blocks for state anchors) and B309 (B285/B287 incomplete: a server naming the largest length held every pass's first turn). The rest LOW, among them a regression of B296 (B305) and a test whose premise B274 changed (B33's witness). No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, or to make an honest member sign twice.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

And a clippy lint in round 17's B293 (a collapsible `if`), now a let-chain.

## Round 19: the audit of 2026-10-07 (five independent read-only reviews of the round-18 tree)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, an adversarial review of round 18's fixes, and (new this round) cryptography, data availability and the bridges, which no round since round 5 had reviewed. Four HIGH: B314 (one hot validator key minted any amount of wBTC, the only asset AIN trades against, and fed the DePIN oracle alone), B315 (B49 incomplete: Move values were priced and held by move-vm's legacy size, which counts a vector of empty vectors as 2 units), B317 (a member leaving at an epoch boundary could not send the vote a block of its last epoch needed: a permanent halt) and B318 (B300 incomplete, the fourth fix of the returning-member family: at an epoch's first rounds the frontier ran past a returning member's lead, and certificates minted past it starved its requests). Six MEDIUM: B312 (B299 incomplete: an answer whose requester closed its connection was never charged), B316 (unpriced type work in the gas meter), B319 (B309/B301 incomplete, found by two reviewers: one session still pinned the parted-fetch budget, and with seven members above a node no large block ever arrived, attacker or not), B320 (QCs were not bound to the genesis), B321 (ML-DSA was verified by three code paths across CPUs) and B313's mempool half. The rest LOW. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, or to make an honest member sign twice.

## Round 20: the audit of 2026-10-07 (five independent read-only reviews of the round-19 tree, uncompiled)

Execution and economics, network and RPC, consensus and sync, cryptography, data availability and the bridges, and an adversarial review of round 19's fixes; four of its findings were also found while reviewing round 19's code before the reviewers ran. One CRITICAL, B343 (no bound on how deep a Move value nests in memory: a chain of structs packed ~20,000 deep for ~0.12M gas, and the recursion of move-vm's copy, comparison and drop, and the meter's size, overflowed the stack on every node: found here and by the adversarial reviewer). Three HIGH: B339 and B341 (the meter walked whole values for a flat price: every Pack and Unpack, and a comparison of two references), B344 (B318's regression: bodies past the lead were never asked for, and after a long run of skipped anchors a member coming back could not decide the block waiting on its vote: a permanent halt with f withholding votes, found by two reviewers). Five MEDIUM: B340 (direct calls of the vector natives took pushed values off the memory quota), B345 (B320's regression: every checkpoint restore failed, found by four reviewers), B346 (a first resource access was flat however long its type's name, and the session's cache uncounted), B347 (B319's shard server rebuilt a block's whole extension on every switch of height) and B348 (a transaction's signature binds its chain id, not its genesis). The rest LOW. No reviewer found a way for honest nodes to commit different blocks or disagree on state, or to make an honest member sign twice.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B339 | HIGH | B246/B315's class: `packed_heap` computed `units(f) - heap(f)` for every field of every Pack and Unpack, walking each container field whole, twice, for a flat 5 + n/4 gas: a loop taking apart and rebuilding a struct around a value of ~250,000 nodes (within the quota) walked a million nodes an iteration, hours of every validator's CPU for one 10M-gas transaction | `core/vm_move/src/gas.rs` 283-287, 437-456; move-vm `interpreter.rs` 1870-1911 (found reviewing round 19; round-20 execution and adversarial reviews) | A pack holds `ABS_STRUCT` and each non-container field's size, read from the field's top (`is_container`): Aptos holds a pack by its fields' abstract stack size | OPEN |
| B340 | MEDIUM | B213/B227 incomplete: a hand-made module could call the `std::vector` natives as functions (the compiler emits their opcodes, but they are registered `public native`), and the meter credits a native's by-value arguments: `push_back` took the pushed value off the memory quota while the vector kept it (~42 units a gas past the quota, ~0.9 GB a 10M-gas transaction), and `push_back`/`pop_back` walked whole values for a flat price | `core/vm_move/src/gas.rs` 358-369, 633-642; `vm_move/src/lib.rs` 199-207, 259; move-stdlib `vector.rs` 96-115 (found reviewing round 19; round-20 execution and adversarial reviews) | The eight `#[bytecode_instruction]` natives are registered as stand-ins that abort (`BYTECODE_ONLY_NATIVE`, charged); they cannot be dropped: the loader refuses a module declaring a native it does not know | OPEN |
| B341 | HIGH | B49/B315 incomplete: `Eq` and `Neq` on two references cost 40 units each (`units` stops at a reference), while move-vm compares the full values behind them: comparisons of references to a value near the quota walked ~10 MB for 3 gas, over 10 minutes of every validator for one 10M-gas transaction | `gas.rs` 107-110, 473-493; move-vm `values_impl.rs` 499-555 (round-20 execution and adversarial reviews) | `Eq`/`Neq` priced by the sizes behind the references (`deref_units`, Aptos's `abstract_value_size_dereferenced`) | OPEN |
| B342 | LOW | B327 incomplete: the VM printed every aborted payload's reason, which carries the caller's names verbatim (~49 KB a line through `aincore_estimateGas`, which checks no signature), outside the executor's quiet estimates; and a stranger drove a log line for every third identity refused on a host | `vm_move/src/lib.rs` 488; `executor/src/lib.rs` 5404-5414; `p2p.rs` 832 (round-20 network review) | The VM prints nothing; the executor logs the reason through its quiet `log!`, cut to 200 characters; the refused identity is logged only when tracing | OPEN |
| B343 | CRITICAL | No bound on how deep a Move value nests in memory: this move-vm bounds depth only where it builds a layout (storage, arguments: `VALUE_DEPTH_MAX`), and a non-generic struct is a one-node type, so 100 chained modules of 200 structs each (`S_i { f: S_(i+1) }`) packed ~20,000 levels for ~0.12M gas; move-vm's copy, comparison and drop, and the meter's size, recurse a frame or two a level, so the stack overflowed and aborted every node re-executing the block, at every restart: a permanent halt (unconfirmed: overflow depth not measured) | `core/executor/src/deps.rs` 35 (limits passed: 200 structs, 256 type nodes, dependency depth 100); `gas.rs` 64-143, 283-287; executor `lib.rs` 3924 (rayon's 2 MiB stacks); move-vm `loader.rs` 2541-2542 (found reviewing round 19; round-20 adversarial review) | At publish, every struct a bundle defines has a depth formula (`deps::check_struct_depths`, through the bundle and the chain): refused when it nests past 128 (Aptos's `max_value_nest_depth`) or adds more than 4 to a type argument's depth, so with move-vm's 256-deep runtime types no value nests past 1,152 levels; witnessed sized, copied, compared and dropped on a 2 MiB stack in an unoptimized build | OPEN |
| B344 | HIGH | B318 regression (found by two reviewers): bodies past this node's lead were never asked for. After a long run of skipped anchors (f members attesting all but the leaders'), the frontier ran far past a member's cursor with no pending QC to bound it; the member coming back fetched every certificate but no body above its lead, could not decide the block the others then decided on its vote, and with f withholding finality votes QC(h) never formed: a permanent halt. ## Round 21: the audit of 2026-10-07 (three independent read-only reviews of the round-20 tree, uncompiled)

An adversarial review of round 20's fixes, execution and the Move VM, and consensus and sync. One CRITICAL, B357, found while re-reading B343's fix before the reviewers reported and then by two of them independently: B343's depth check itself recursed past any thread's stack. Consensus and sync found one MEDIUM and two LOW. The reviewers found the rest of round 20 sound: B343's depth bound, B339/B341/B346's meter, B340's natives, B354's binding on every producer and verifier, and every QC store path verifying first.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B357 | CRITICAL | B343 incomplete, its own fix: `check_struct_depths` recursed a frame per struct (`of_struct`) and per type level (`of_token`), and learned a depth only on the way back; the only bound was 257 open structs, and a field may nest 256 levels (the deserializer's `SIGNATURE_TOKEN_DEPTH_MAX`). One module of 257 structs, each holding the next inside 255 vectors (~70 KB, under `MAX_TX_BYTES`), recursed ~66,000 frames before any bound was checked, past a rayon worker's 2 MiB stack: a SIGSEGV, not a panic, on every node executing the block, again at every restart. It runs before the bytecode verifier, and admission never parses modules | `core/executor/src/deps.rs` 308-379, 407-441; executor `lib.rs` 5813-5819 under `par_iter` 3929 (found reviewing B343; round-21 adversarial and execution reviews) | The definitions are walked from an explicit stack (enter, then exit after the structs a struct's fields name), each struct's depth checked as soon as it is resolved, the deepest first; a token's own nesting (at most 256) is the only recursion; struct definitions indexed per module (a struct was found by a linear scan) | OPEN |
| B358 | MEDIUM | B166 incomplete: pending QC work was retried on every reload of the chain tip, i.e. on every pass of the commit loop, i.e. once per vertex that grew O_E (~n a round), up to 8 rows each, every row reading its whole block, parsing it and recomputing every root (a Reed-Solomon extension included) under the storage writer gate, then sending its partial vote to every member: with f members withholding their votes, ~8n block validations and 8n vote broadcasts a round on every node, and past n ~150 a member's resends alone exceeded `MEMBER_MSGS_PER_SEC`, so receivers dropped its pushes, vertices included | `dag.rs` 833-835, 984, 1949, 1517-1518; `qc_producer/recovery.rs` 9, 99-133, 152; `sessions.rs` 787 (round-21 consensus review) | Retried round-robin once a tick; the row a block creation or adoption just staged is worked on alone, at once (`qc_work_at`); a retry checks the held block's header (its hash recomputed) and anchor, read without the rest of the body, which was checked when the work was staged (`held_header`) | OPEN |
| B359 | LOW | Every uncertified own proposal was broadcast whole to every member every tick until GC, against PR-4 (only to members missing from the collector) and RC-1 step 6 (only rounds at or above the current round - 1): a member whose vertices nobody attests (f withholding, one honest member away) pushed ~100 bodies of up to 768 KiB a tick, past its byte budget, and its new vertices were dropped with them | `v4.rs` 1730-1734, 649-672; `v4/gc.rs` 165; `docs/G1_CONSENSUS_CONTRACT.md` 519, 1308 (round-21 consensus review) | Only the last two rounds' uncertified proposals go again, each to the staked members whose attestation it lacks (`CertCollector::missing`) | OPEN |
| B360 | LOW | B356 incomplete: a shard turn's pace was measured over the whole turn, its busy waits included, so an honest server answering busy after a piece (B347: three times a build, ~210 s on a Pi at 512 MiB) was struck as slow | `sync/src/lib.rs` 1665-1676, 1716-1720, 2423-2428 (round-21 consensus review) | The pace is over the time the session served: the busy answers' waits are left out | OPEN |

## Round 24: the audit of 2026-10-07 (a workflow of 30 read-only agents over the round-23 tree, uncompiled)

An adversarial review of round 23, a sweep of the stake measures (B374's class), a review of a node coming back, and a review of state sync and restore; each finding checked by two or three agents trying to refute it. No CRITICAL or HIGH; three MEDIUM (B387, B388, B389), nine LOW; one reported LOW was refuted by both verifiers (`--restore-validator-key` keeps an existing key on purpose, B373's mode).

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B387 | MEDIUM | B376's regression: a busy answer ended the turn and dropped the session's unfinished shard (only proved shards were kept), so under the server's answer budget (about 65 tokens a 2 MiB piece, 12.8 a second) a shard of two pieces or more never finished: every block whose body passed 256 MiB (128 data shards) stalled every lagging node, unstruck | `sync/src/lib.rs` 1683, 1724-1731; `sessions.rs` 918-919, 989, 1104 (round-24 adversarial review of round 23) | Each session's unfinished shard (index, bytes, the length it claimed) is kept in the fetch and resumed at that session's next turn only (a liar spoils only its own), eight at most | OPEN |
| B388 | MEDIUM | A node behind the members' retention never synced: block turns go to members first whatever they hold, three a pass, so members that pruned below its next block took every turn and an archive non-member that held the blocks was never asked; a validator could not restore either (SN-6) | `sync/src/lib.rs` 243-250, 1290, 1483-1503, 2508-2545 (round-24 liveness review) | A session that showed it no longer holds this node's next block (a prune horizon above it, or an answer starting above it) goes after every other session for `STRIKE_TTL` | OPEN |
| B389 | MEDIUM | B374 incomplete: the protected list was written only at boundaries, so a member that joined during an epoch, or whose pool grew in it, was still displaced by own stake alone before the next boundary elected it, its pool's weight with it | `staking.move` 233-262; executor `lib.rs` 2732-2758, 790-829 (round-24 stake-measure sweep; one verifier rated it LOW) | Before a join into a full Move set, the protected list is staged with the weights as they stand (own stake, open pool and bootstrap weight, as the next boundary would elect: `protected_now`) | OPEN |
| B390 | LOW | B371's trade-off: a node whose ordering a recorded conflict halts keeps importing and executing blocks through sync, while pruning holds every block above its adoption cursor: its disk grew without end, unnoticed behind a working RPC | `dag.rs` 2345-2354, 2012; `sync/src/lib.rs` 1056-1065 (round-24 adversarial review of round 23) | Sync stops while a conflict alarm is recorded (`recorded_conflict`), as it does for `sync:halt_reason` | OPEN |

An adversarial review of round 24, a determinism review and a V4 safety review; each finding checked by two agents trying to refute it. No CRITICAL, HIGH or MEDIUM; eight LOW (one verifier rated B399 MEDIUM). Two reviews reported B402; it is one row.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B399 | LOW | B388 missed one exit: a server whose first held block above the requester is larger than a batch answered with no blocks and that block named oversized, and the client's oversized branch broke without noting it pruned, so pruned members kept their member-first turns ahead of the archive node holding the blocks | `sync/src/lib.rs` 1508-1512; server 2598-2641 (round-25 adversarial review of round 24) | The oversized branch notes the server pruned when the named block is past the requester's next | OPEN |
| B400 | LOW | B389's refresh (the whole Move set decoded twice, a v1 parse, a pool read a member, a sort) was paid by no gas and ran for every join into a full set, a join with no stake included, which Move refuses at once; its write outlived the abort. About 445 such joins fit a block, a few seconds of unpaid work on every node | executor `lib.rs` 5104-5128, 1030-1058, 790-829, 5855 (round-25 adversarial review of round 24) | The refresh runs only for a join that can displace someone: its stake at least the minimum and held by the sender (two point reads) | OPEN |
| B401 | LOW | B397's restore kept a full copy of an oversized block per answering peer for the whole download, re-extended (Reed-Solomon) each answer although its body was proved once, and dropped every proved shard on any error, refusal or busy spell, so a 22-minute fetch could restart from shard 0 again and again | `sync/src/state_sync.rs` 1003-1098, 1153-1180, 1796, 1916 (round-25 adversarial review of round 24) | One shard fetch for the whole restore (`AnchorBody`), extended by every peer's turn; a turn that ends short keeps every proved shard, and its peer's unfinished one unless it lied (B387); a peer still busy ends its turn, and rounds that proved a shard or met a busy peer wait within the deadline. The body's checks run once, when its last shard proves; pairs share one `Arc` body beside their own signatures, the signature is checked on the header alone, and the whole block is built for the verified pair only | OPEN |
| B402 | LOW | B394 incomplete: `qc_want_peers` sorted members and every other session into one rotation, so 50 inbound stranger sessions that never answer stretched a member's turn about 25 times (a sync-only QC, and with it an activation, waited minutes) | `sessions.rs` 2151-2183; `p2p.rs` 377-386 (round-25 adversarial and V4 safety reviews) | The groups are kept apart: each turn asks two members (B198's rotation) and one other session, two others when no member is connected | OPEN |
| B403 | LOW | B393 scaled the 80 % fee pool by W / max(W, s_min) but not the 20 % leader bonus; leaders are drawn by stake, so below s_min a survivor led w / W of the blocks and its bonus income rose by s_min / W after a rival's forfeit. BW-7 still said fees were unchanged | executor `lib.rs` 3404-3435; `ordering.rs` 1372-1376; `G5_ECONOMICS_CONTRACT.md` 574 (round-25 adversarial review of round 24) | The bonus is scaled as the pool is and the rest burned with it; BW-7 states the floor and the burn | OPEN |
| B404 | LOW | Block-content inputs read outside the block transaction turned a storage error into data: the genesis-identity read in evidence verification dropped the item, and the parent block's time fell back to the in-memory running max, so one transient read error built a header no other node built (a decision conflict, then a halt) | `dag.rs` 1120-1127, 1659-1666; executor `lib.rs` 4545-4549, 4666-4670 (round-25 determinism review) | The genesis-identity read fails closed as the committee, jail and retention reads do; the parent time (`parent_timestamp`) is no decision on a read error or a corrupt row and the anchor is deferred. The read-error path has no witness (storage has no failure injection); the corrupt row has one | OPEN |
| B405 | LOW | RC-3's resume point came from the node's own clock, so a wiped validator booting with a clock behind (a Pi before NTP) re-armed its signing guards at activations it replayed while catching up, inside an epoch its old self had signed in: a twin proposal (slashed) or a second attestation | `v4.rs` 462-466; `v4/epoch.rs` 389-402, 635-653; `dag.rs` 2133 (round-25 V4 safety review) | No clock and no peer's word: while it abstains, the node sends a proof-of-boot transaction (a transfer to itself of a random amount, its bytes new) about once a minute, forwarded like a non-member's; the first time one has a receipt it notes the height it holds (`note_boot_proof`, kept once); it re-arms at an activation whose boundary block is above that height. Open clears any earlier proof (`begin_abstaining`). An abstaining member forwards its mempool (`v4_signs`). Two earlier designs (a time point at activations; a height judged level from members' claims) failed the round-26 review | OPEN |
| B406 | LOW | B390 incomplete: sync stopped on decision-conflict and committee-mismatch alarms but not on CE-3's certificate-conflict alarm, so a node it halted kept importing blocks it would never adopt or prune. One HIGH (B422, a class no round since 11 had reviewed), two MEDIUM reported by two reviewers as one (B421), six LOW. Two LOW were refuted by one verifier and confirmed by the other (B423, B429).

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |

An adversarial review of rounds 27-28, a sweep of the Move stdlib modules users call, and a sweep of the DA layer with the block execution and import path; each finding checked by one or two agents trying to refute it. Three HIGH: two in round-27/28 fixes (B430's pool, B422's bound) and one in sync's batch import. The DEX finding was rated MEDIUM by its reviewer and LOW by its verifier.
