## Round 29 audit (2026-10-15: nine read-only area reviews of the round-28 tree, each finding checked by three verifiers)

Nine read-only reviews of the round-28 tree: an adversarial review of round 28's diff, consensus, sync, the executor, the Move VM, the Move economic modules, transaction ingress, DA with networking and crypto, and a sweep of the whole tree for the B150 class; each finding checked by three verifiers with distinct lenses (the code path, the scenario, the ledger and its severity). Nineteen findings were confirmed; two pairs are one defect each (B443, B447), so with B458 there are eighteen rows. B458 is CRITICAL: the vote rule first counted it refuted, two of three, because one verifier stopped inconclusive; an attacker and a defender then adjudicated it, and it was confirmed: a phantom or value-less type parameter drops B343's depth step (the step the attacker's bound rested on), so move-vm's layout arguments grew 2^k, unmetered, the same on every node. Its first fix, a per-position multiplier, was broken by review and replaced by an affine bound; a later review found the total layout work per gas and an entry function's return values unbounded, now refused at publish too (round 29b). Verified severities: one CRITICAL, four MEDIUM and thirteen LOW, one of them (B450) ACCEPTED; the fix reviews added B459 (MEDIUM, a pre-existing operator-guide hazard). Two claims the verifiers refuted have no row: that B435's ~576 type nodes a gas leaves out the paranoid checks' compare and ability walks (B435 counts the nodes those checks build, says so, and is right in that unit; the larger count does not change its conclusion), and that whole stored blocks are parsed where the header suffices (sync 1101: no one can make a node do more than one extra parse per honest block, and one of the three sites runs outside the writer gate; B443 moved sync's two to `header_of` anyway). The fixes were written without a compiler and were not yet compiled when this section was written; they are compiled and tested on the NAS next.

| ID | Severity | Bug | Evidence | Plan | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B441 | MEDIUM | RC-3 abstention hit every honest joiner for its whole first member epoch. No guard origin is written at a first key use, although G1's key table said one was. Only a member of C_E sent the proof of boot that re-arms a node, so a joiner's proof landed in its first member epoch and it re-armed only at the next. G5 CH-1 (10% of C_E's stake an epoch) makes the finding's no-attacker halt unreachable. For that epoch, though, the fault margin fell from under 1/3 to about 23-24%: crashes or a Byzantine coalition of that size stalled it until they resumed, and each joiner lost its first member epoch's vertices, leader slots and leader bonus, though its stake-weighted share of emission and fees was still paid (finding HIGH; verifiers MEDIUM, MEDIUM, LOW) | `dag.rs` prove_boot, gated on `abstains`; `v4.rs` abstains, can_sign; `v4/epoch.rs` activate_next; G1 key table; the operator guide and `scripts/validator-join` gave no join timing (round-29 consensus review) | A node without continuous guards seeks its proof while its key is in C_E or in the live set that C_{E+1} is derived from (`Engine::seeks_boot_proof`, reading the live set only when a proof is due; `lacks_guards` gates the receipt check). A joiner therefore proves during the epoch it joined in, and E+1's activation re-arms it. B405's safety argument is unchanged: the block that carries the proof was made after this boot. C_{E+1} is derived from the live set, so asking it as well adds nothing. G1 RC-3 now gives the join procedure, and its key table no longer claims a first-key-use origin. The operator side gives the same procedure (`docs/NODE_OPERATOR_GUIDE.md` "Stake Tokens and Join", `scripts/validator-join/join-validator.sh` and its README): keep the node running and synced from the join until it signs (every start before then clears a noted proof; the guide's Maintenance section says so for its restarts too), register with the node's own key, fund the stake plus a little AIN for the join's and the proof's gas (1,010 AIN, as `fund-validator.sh` sends; `join` checks at least 1,001, so a join that aborts after paying its gas, e.g. `ECHURN_LIMIT`, can be retried without a top-up), join early in an epoch (`join` refuses in an epoch's second half unless `AINCORE_JOIN_LATE=1`: I is at least 100 blocks, so at least 50 remain for a proof sent once a minute; given `AINCORE_REFERENCE_RPC`, it also refuses while the node trails that node by more than 3 blocks and times the epoch from the higher height, and without it warns that it reads the local height), and the proof must be noted below H_E (the node's `[RC-3]` line names the height it resumes above). Witness `a_joiner_proves_its_boot_before_its_first_member_epoch`: a fifth node is in the live set only and has no origin; it proves in epoch 0, notes a height below H_0, is re-armed at C_1's activation, and its vertices stage. Residuals: a proof noted at or above H_E, the boundary of the epoch its join landed in (a join late in that epoch), still re-arms one epoch later (a join that lands after H_E is first a member in E+2, and its proof has all of E+1 to land), and a restart before E+1's activation, not only before H_E, clears a noted proof; a late genesis starter is B370's accepted launch window | OPEN |
| B442 | MEDIUM | Every certificate naming a parent of a body kept PENDING woke that body and re-authenticated it in full, uncounted: Layer S over up to 768 KiB (two serializations, the payload hash, the signature). Every certificate also took the whole buffer out, sorted it and scanned each held vertex's refs. A member that signed, each round, a body citing honest round r-1 digests before their certificates formed cost every honest node about f(n-f) Layer S runs a round under the consensus lock (~5 s a round at n = 50 on a Pi). This made B169/B224's 'one authentication per distinct body' false, and honest catch-up on stripped pulled bodies could pay the same (B224/B257 class; three verifiers MEDIUM; one moved the attack from an invented parent to honest parents only, which runs every round) | `v4.rs` ingest_cert wake, on_vertex; `staging.rs` PendingBuffer take_all; `ingress_v4.rs` Layer S (round-29 consensus review) | The buffer indexes each held vertex by the parent slots it waits on (`push_waiting`, from the verdict's missing refs) and how many are left. A certificate takes out only the vertices whose last awaited parent it is (`certified`). Each is judged once more (`wake`, uncounted as B68 needs), without Layer S and without a re-serialization for its length (`v4_verdict_judged`: the digest binds every field Layer S reads, and activation empties the buffer). GC prunes in place (`prune_to`), and every removal drops the vertex's waits. Witnesses: `a_pending_body_is_judged_again_once_without_layer_s` (three stripped round-2 bodies: the one whose parents arrive is judged again once with no Layer S run; the one with a junk payload is refused at its wake, uncharged to the certificate; the one citing an invented parent is never judged again) and `a_waiting_vertex_is_woken_once_by_its_last_parent` (the index through removal, pruning, draining and eviction). B68's witness `a_waking_certificate_is_not_charged_for_the_vertex_it_wakes` waits on real parents again (its junk only on their certificates, stripped, the body re-kept with `push_waiting`): the honest member's certificate comes last and wakes it, and the payload refused at that wake costs it nothing (after the index change its body waited on invented parents, so no certificate woke it and the check passed vacuously) | OPEN |
| B443 | LOW | B355 and B433 were incomplete (B229's class, on finality QCs). A QC for a held block awaiting its QC that failed its stake checks (no signer, a bit past the committee, a wrong stake, below the quorum) read the whole block under the writer gate and was refused uncharged, with a log line. A sync session's QC copying a public QC's fields over another vote, with any signature, read the block whole before its pairing failed, and sync struck nobody for it: every session sync asks for finality (up to three level sessions, and each turn's answers and its closing GET_FINALITY) could send one every pass. A QC naming another epoch than its height's (any height) and a malformed `QC_VOTE` (its text echoed, up to 8 KiB) were logged uncharged, so B51 never muted the sender. Every stored QC read and parsed its block three times. Sync's admission parsed each imported block's whole parent, and its already-executed branch parsed the whole stored block, only for a hash (findings #2 and #5; all six verifiers LOW) | `qc_producer.rs` stage_certificate, check_held_block, store_certificate; `qc.rs` verify_stake_aggregate; `dag.rs` on_boundary_qc, handle_remote_qc_vote; `sync/src/lib.rs` the callers of apply_finality_artifact (1392-1403, 1713, 1721, 1804, 1825-1833), validate_admission_in 1098-1102, the AlreadyExecuted branch 2388-2394 (round-29 consensus and sync reviews) | `stage_certificate` now verifies before it reads the block: the stake checks and the pairing, with any failure charged once (`verify_qc_charged`). This comes after `may_hold_block`, which is exact once a block is held, so an unheld height still costs no pairing (B101). On a V4 chain a node with no tip (before its first block: genesis writes none, and every block is written with its tip) takes no QC before the committee lookup: `may_hold_block` holds for any height there, and with the pairing first each replayed QC cost it one. It also charges an epoch mismatch, and reads and parses the block once (`put_certificate` stores without reading it again). A malformed vote is charged and logged without its text; a refused `QC_CERT` is logged cut to 200 characters with control characters escaped (`loggable`, B342/B448's cut): its error echoes fields the sender chose (a claimed chain id or committee hash, up to 64 KiB), line breaks included. Sync counts the checks a session's finality QC failed (`finality_lied`: `consensus::work::failed_in` around `apply_finality_artifact`), and any failure is that session's lie. A level session is struck. A turn's session is refused (`turn_finality`: struck whatever else the turn did), and the turn ends at the answer that carried the QC. Admission and the already-executed branch read the stored block's header alone (`header_of`). Witnesses: `a_qc_failing_its_signature_checks_is_refused_charged_before_the_block_is_read` (V4: four junk QCs, each refused before the block is read and charged once; a valid one reads the block once); `a_v4_node_before_its_first_block_pairs_no_qc` (with a control that pairs and stores once the block and tip are held); `a_vote_from_outside_the_committee_is_charged` (extended to malformed votes); `a_refused_qc_is_logged_cut_and_escaped`; `a_level_session_whose_finality_qc_fails_is_struck` (the real QC is the control); `a_turns_finality_qc_that_fails_strikes_its_session` (a lie in an answer ends the turn and strikes; a lie in the closing GET_FINALITY strikes; honest finality is the control); `a_lie_in_a_parted_answers_finality_ends_the_turn`; `admission_reads_the_parents_header_alone`; `an_executed_heights_stored_block_is_known_by_its_header`. B101's witness now sets the tip, as every node that holds a block has one. Residuals: (1) On a legacy V3 datadir, a node before its first block (no `latest_height`) still pays a pairing for a QC naming any height. (2) Each forged QC still costs one pairing under the writer gate before it is refused, because `import_finality_qc` verifies inside its transaction. That is at most about nine a pass (up to three level sessions, and two a turn: the closing `GET_FINALITY` is still asked after a refused turn), each striking its session, and a member ranks first whatever its strikes. (3) Level sessions are asked in order of membership and salt, not strikes, so a struck one is asked again the next pass. (4) A lie in an empty answer's finality changes the verdict only after a full batch, because an empty answer from a session above this node is struck anyway; that case has no witness | OPEN |
| B444 | MEDIUM | B41 incomplete: sync checked a block's body roots, including the DA root's full Reed-Solomon extension, before it looked up the block's QC. A Byzantine member could answer each pass with one self-signed block of about 9 MiB at the requester's next height and no QC. Changing one byte a pass defeats the root cache, and a wrong DA root cost the full extension all the same. Every validator and observer paid about 1 s of all-core CPU a pass, and the member's turn ended well at a QC-less tip. B41 moved only the signature ahead of the roots, and a member's own signature costs it nothing | `sync/src/lib.rs` validate_block_in 1039, process_blocks_with_qcs 2291-2318 (round-29 sync review; three verifiers, all MEDIUM) | The import now runs the checks that need no QC first (`validate_header_in`: height, parent, eligible proposer and signer, signature, anchor binding). On V4 it then checks the block's QC (`verify_block_qc`: the field binds, then one pairing). It checks the body's roots (`check_commitments`: the transaction, vertex and evidence hashes and the DA extension) only under a QC that verified. For a block without its QC, the body's roots, and so the DA extension, are never computed. Only `validate_header_in` reads the body, and it reads three things: each slash-evidence item's kind (the item parsed whole), the transaction count and the last committed vertex. A body that does not match a certified header ends the turn badly, so the session is struck unless a full batch came first. Admission's `validate_block_in` still runs every check. The other `check_commitments` callers need no change: state-sync restore (B398) works on checkpoint-bound headers, and pending-QC recovery and the V3 finality import work on held blocks. Witness `v4_a_blocks_body_is_checked_only_under_its_verified_qc`. Residuals: a member, which ranks first, can still cost each node the answer's ~10 MiB download, its parse and its size count every pass; and a turn that ends at the member's own QC-less block still ends well (B445's residual) | OPEN |
| B445 | LOW | B274 incomplete: a block offered without its QC ended a turn well whatever the block was. A session that answered junk at the requester's next height, or relayed the real block without its QC, had its strikes cleared every turn without any identity churn, and outranked any honest non-member server holding one strike. The parted path also ended a turn well on any header named without a QC, before `check_named` ran | `sync/src/lib.rs` sync_over 1667-1671, 1789-1794, 1808-1814 (round-29 sync review; verifiers rated it LOW twice and MEDIUM once, and showed that the reported fix, every check but the QC, still leaves the relay open) | A QC-less block now ends a turn well (`qcless_tip`) only when two things hold: the import stopped at it for want of its QC alone (`Imported::qcless`, so it passed `validate_header_in`), and the session is a committee member. On the parted path, only a member's named block whose header passes `check_named` without a QC ends the turn well. An honest non-member never offers a block that checks without its QC: every block it synced is stored with its QC, and its own copies carry no eligible signer. Any other QC-less stop ends the turn like an empty answer, so the session is struck unless a full batch came first. Witnesses `a_qcless_block_ends_a_turn_well_only_as_a_members_own_work` (a member's real block is the control) and `an_oversized_block_without_its_qc_ends_a_turn_well_only_as_a_members_own`. Residuals: a member can answer every turn with a block it signed itself at the requester's next height and no QC, so it is never struck and goes before struck honest members. This is inherent: a member can sign any header, so nothing tells that block from an honest member's newest, QC-less one (B274/B289). (1) Carried arguments: a struct whose layout could carry, below it, a struct instantiation whose type arguments hold more than `MAX_LAYOUT_ARGUMENT_NODES` (1,024) nodes together, given its own arguments of `MAX_INSTANTIATED_ARGUMENT_NODES` (192) each. `Depths` resolves an affine bound per struct (`Carried`: a scale per type parameter and a constant) through the bundle and the chain alongside the depth (`carried_of_token`): all the positions of one nested instantiation are summed, each scaled by the nested struct's resolved scale there (at least 1) plus its constant and every constant node on the way; instantiations inside arguments count as if laid out; max across fields and instantiations; saturating. (2) Round 29b, the total work: a struct whose layout builds more than `MAX_LAYOUT_WORK_NODES` = 16 x 576 = 9,216 type nodes, its arguments 192 nodes together. `Work` (the same affine form, `work_of_token`/`work_of_instance`, resolved alongside `Carried`) counts, for each instantiation the layout lays out, its arguments (cloned for the loader's cache key and hashed twice) and each of its fields substituted, plus what each field lays out (a vector's element once, an instantiation inside an argument as if laid out, a non-generic struct its own constant), summed over fields and argument positions, each nested struct's resolved work composed with its arguments' sizes; saturating. The cap is B422's accepted ratio, 576 type nodes a gas (a generic `CopyLoc` of a 576-node local; an unpack and pack), times the cheapest first access, 16 gas (`exists` of an absent resource: `load_base` 10 + `exists` 5 + its name, at least 34 bytes at 42 a gas, 1): a root's own layout now builds at most 576 unmetered type nodes a gas, B422's ratio (before: ~1M nodes for 16 gas, ~60,000 a gas). Evaluated with the arguments together (the most-scaled parameter takes all 192), not each at 192 as `Carried` is: every root's arguments hold at most 127 nodes together, and each at 192 would refuse a Liquidswap event store (9,824). Accepted: the stdlib's maximum 582 (`dex::LiquidityPool`: 3 x 192 + 6; `coin::CoinStore` 578, `option::Option` 385, `Coin<C>` 193; witnessed exactly in `the_stdlib_structs_are_within_the_depth_bounds`), a Liquidswap event store of 8 `Q<X,Y,Curve,u64>` 3,296 (~4,900 with real `EventHandle<E<X,Y,Curve>>`s), the terminal reach-4 wrapper `P<T>{vector<Q<T,T,T,T>>}` 1,731, B422's widest field `Q<T,T,T,vector^57<T>>` 1,844. (3) Round 29b: an entry function whose return signature is non-empty (`check_type_work`; Aptos refuses these; none of the stdlib's 47 entry functions returns a value, read from its bytecode): move-vm substitutes its return types with the transaction's type arguments and lays them out, a root whose arguments a return type may name 4 times, past the premise both bounds rest on. Sound by induction: an instantiation laid out either comes from a definition token, whose struct's bounds compose its children's, or from the root's own arguments (at most 127 nodes together: `instantiate_generic_type` and `instantiate_generic_function` checks, struct instantiations of reach 1; entry arguments hold no struct), each within its own struct's bounds. Witnesses: `a_layout_multiplying_chain_is_refused` (Carried: phantom doubling chain 3 levels accepted / 4 refused 1,543, two-parameter sum chain 2 / 3 1,542, alternating one-parameter chain 2 / 3 pairs 1,543, constants scaled by the struct below 2 / 3 levels 1,013 / 1,257 (replacing the additive chain, now refused first by the work bound); terminal wrapper 768, B422's wide field 825 and the event store 577 accepted), `a_layout_building_too_many_nodes_is_refused` (Work: `W<A>` of n fields `vector<B_j<A>>`, distinct `B_j<A>{vector<Q<A,A,A,A>>}` each carrying 768, 4 fields build 7,892 accepted / 5 refused 9,817; the additive chain `L_i<A>{vector<L_{i+1}<P<A,vector^59<u8>>>>}` 7 levels 7,504 / 8 refused 9,363 while carrying only 619; terminal wrapper 1,731, wide field 1,844, event store 3,296 exact), `an_entry_function_returning_a_value_is_refused` (an entry function returning a `u64` refused; entry returning nothing and a public function returning a value accepted). Residual (OPEN): move-vm still builds the layout before any charge (B298/B346 class; the bound caps the work a gas, it does not meter it); each struct instantiation in a root's own type arguments is laid out too, each within its own struct's bounds but priced only by the root's name (at least 34 bytes, under a gas, each), so a root whose arguments hold s of them may build (1 + s) x 9,216 nodes for 15 + ceil(34 x (1 + s) / 42) gas, up to ~11,000 a gas as s grows (~20x B422), fewer once move-vm's 256 layout nodes run out: pricing a first access's struct nodes at ~16 gas each (`gas.rs` owner) or a substituted-node meter in `struct_gidx_to_type_layout` (move-vm owner) closes it; modules stored before this check are not re-checked (it binds from a fresh genesis) | OPEN |
| B459 | MEDIUM | The operator guide's Backup section archived a validator's whole data directory and never warned against restoring it. Restoring is the natural use of a backup, and under G1 SA-4 it is Byzantine: the restored `guard_origin` matches the node, so its guards count as continuous and it signs again over rounds it signed after the backup was taken, an equivocation slashed 100% (with its delegators). An honest operator following the official guide could lose the whole stake | `docs/NODE_OPERATOR_GUIDE.md` Backup (pre-existing; found by the round-29 B441 docs review) | The Backup section now says a validator never restores its database, backs up only `node.key` (offline, encrypted), and recovers from an empty data directory with its key and a fresh sync, which abstains under RC-3 until its proof of boot lands (as "Database Corruption" already did); a full archive is only for a node that is not a validator. Residual: nothing in the node refuses a restored validator database (G1 SA-4 leaves it to the operator) | OPEN |
